## I / 第一章
===Original===

Observations on the Causes of War

Felix, qui potuit rerum cognoscere causas.

> Virg.

In the attempt to form an accurate estimate of the moral character of human actions and opinions, it is often of importance to inquire how they have been produced. There is always great reason to doubt the rectitude of that, of which the causes and motives are impure; and if, therefore, it should appear from the observations which follow, that some of the motives to war, and of its causes, are inconsistent with reason or with virtue, I would invite the reader to pursue the inquiry that succeeds them, with suspicion, at least, of the rectitude of our ordinary opinions.

There are some customs which have obtained so generally and so long, that what was originally an effect becomes a cause, and what was a cause becomes an effect, until, by the reciprocal influence of each, the custom is continued by circumstances so multiplied and involved, that it is difficult to detect them in all their ramifications, or to determine those to which it is principally to be referred.

What were once the occasion of wars may be easily supposed﻿—robbery, or the repulsion of robbers, was probably the only motive to hostility, until robbery became refined into ambition, and it was sufficient to produce a war that a chief was not content with the territory of his fathers. But by the gradually increasing complication of society from age to age, and by the multiplication of remote interests and obscure rights, the motives to war have become so numerous and so technical, that ordinary observation often fails to perceive what they are. They are sometimes known only to a cabinet, which is influenced in its decision by reasonings of which a nation knows little, or by feelings of which it knows nothing: so that of those who personally engage in hostilities, there is, perhaps, not often one in ten who can distinctly tell why he is fighting.

===Chinese===

论战争的起因

能够探寻事物起因的人是幸福的。

> 维吉尔（Virg.）

在试图对人类行为与观念的道德属性作出准确评估时，探究它们是如何产生的往往至关重要。凡起因与动机不纯之事，其正当性总有极大理由令人怀疑；因此，如果在接下来的观察中显明，某些战争的动机与起因与理性或美德相悖，我愿邀请读者在随后的探讨中，至少带着对我们日常观念之正当性的怀疑态度来进行审视。

有些习俗由来已久、流传甚广，以至于最初的果变成了因，最初的因变成了果，直到在二者的相互作用下，习俗在错综复杂、层出不穷的环境中延续下去，使人难以理清其千丝万缕的脉络，也难以断定它主要归因于何者。

战争曾经的缘由不难想见——掠夺，或是击退掠夺者，大概是敌对行动最初唯一的动机；直到掠夺演化为野心，一个首领不满足于父辈留下的领土便足以引发一场战争。然而，随着社会代代愈加复杂，随着间接利益与模糊权利的成倍增加，战争的动机已变得如此繁杂、如此具有技术性，以至于常人的观察往往难以察觉其究竟何在。它们有时唯有内阁知晓，而内阁做出决断所受的推论，举国上下知之甚少，内阁所凭的情绪，国民更是一无所知：因此，在亲身卷入敌对行动的人当中，往往十个人里难有一人能清楚说出自己究竟因何而战。

===Original===

This refinement in the motives of war is no trifling evidence that they are insufficient or bad. When it is considered how tremendous a battle is, how many it hurries in a moment from the world, how much wretchedness and how much guilt it produces, it would surely appear that nothing but *obvious* necessity should induce us to resort to it. But when, instead of a battle, we have a war with many battles, and of course with multiplied suffering and accumulated guilt, the motives to so dreadful a measure ought to be such as to force themselves upon involuntary observation, and to be written, as it were, in the skies. If, then, a large proportion of a people are often without any distinct perception of the reasons why they are slaughtering mankind, it implies, I think, prima facie evidence against the adequacy or the justice of the motives to slaughter.

It would not, perhaps, be affectation to say, that of the reasons why we so readily engage in war, one of the principal is, that we do not inquire into the subject. We have been accustomed, from earliest life, to a familiarity with all its “pomp and circumstance;” soldiers have passed us at every step, and battles and victories have been the topic of everyone around us. War, therefore, becomes familiarized to all our thoughts, and interwoven with all our associations. We have never inquired whether these things should be; the question does not even suggest itself. We acquiesce in it, as we acquiesce in the rising of the sun, without any other idea than that it is a part of the ordinary process of the world. And how are we to feel disapprobation of a system that we do not examine, and of the nature of which we do not think? Want of inquiry has been the means by which long continued practices, whatever has been their enormity, have obtained the general concurrence of the world, and by which they have continued to pollute or degrade it, long after the few who inquire into their nature have discovered them to be bad. It was by these means that the slave-trade was so long tolerated by this land of humanity. Men did not *think* of its iniquity. We were induced to think, and we soon abhorred and then abolished it. In the present moral state of the world, therefore, I believe it is the business of him who would perceive pure morality, to question the purity of that which now obtains.

===Chinese===

战争动机的这种复杂精巧，恰好证明了这些动机的不充分或不正当。考虑到一场战役是何等可怕，刹那间便让多少人匆匆离世，带来了何等深重的苦难与罪愆，显然唯有显而易见的必要性，才应当促使我们诉诸战争。然而，当我们面对的不是单场战役，而是一场包含诸多战役的战争，并随之带来成倍的痛苦与累积的罪恶时，采取如此可怕措施的动机，理应昭然若揭，宛如书写在苍穹之上。如果一个国家的大多数人往往根本不清楚自己残杀同胞的理由，我认为，这便构成了初步证据，证明这种屠杀的动机既不充分，也不公正。

若说我们之所以如此轻易地卷入战争，其主要原因之一就是我们从未对这一问题进行深究，这或许并不为过。我们自幼便习惯于熟悉战争的一切「威仪与阵势」；军人在我们身旁擦肩而过，战役与胜利是周围每个人口中的谈资。因此，战争潜移默化地融入了我们的一切思绪，与我们的一切联想交织在一起。我们从未探究过这些事情是否合乎情理；这个疑问甚至从未在脑海中闪现。我们默认它，正如我们默认日出一般，除认为它是世间常规进程的一部分之外，别无他想。然而，对于一个我们从未审视过的体制、其本质我们从未深思过的事物，我们又怎能产生反对之意？缺乏审思，正是诸多长期存在的恶行不论何等骇人听闻却能在世间获得普遍认可的根源，也是在少数深究其本质的人发现其危害之后，这些恶行仍能长期玷污或贬损世间的原因。正因如此，奴隶贸易才曾在这片仁道之邦被容忍了如此之久。人们此前并未思考过它的不义。当我们被引导去思考时，很快便对其深恶痛绝，继而予以废除。因此，在世间当前的道德状况下，我相信，凡欲领悟纯正道德之人，其职责正是去质疑当下盛行的一切是否纯洁。

===Original===

“The vices of another age,” says Robertson, “astonish and shock us; the vices of our own become familiar, and excite little horror.”﻿—“The influence of any national custom, both on the understanding, on the heart, and how far it may go towards perverting or extinguishing moral principles of the greatest importance, is remarkable. They who [in 1566] had leisure to reflect and to judge, appear to be no more shocked at the crime of assassination, than the persons who committed it in the heat and impetuosity of passion.”3

Two hundred and fifty years have added something to our morality. We have learned, at least, to abhor assassination; and I am not afraid to hope that the time will arrive when historians shall think of war what Robertson thinks of murder, and shall endeavor, like him, to account for the ferocity and moral blindness of their forefathers. For I do not think the influence of habit in the perversion or extinction of our moral principles, is in any other thing so conspicuous or deplorable as in the subject before us. They who are shocked at a single murder in the highway, hear with indifference of the murder of a thousand on the field. They whom the idea of a single corpse would thrill with terror, contemplate that of heaps of human carcasses, mangled by human hands, with frigid indifference. If a murder is committed, the narrative is given in the public newspaper, with many expressions of commiseration, with many adjectives of horror, and many hopes that the perpetrator will be detected. In the next paragraph, the editor, perhaps, tells us that he has hurried a second edition to the press, in order that he may be the first to glad the public with the intelligence, that in an engagement which has just taken place, *eight hundred and fifty of the enemy were killed*. By war, the natural impulses of the heart seem to be suspended, as if a fiend of blood were privileged to exercise a spell upon our sensibilities, whenever we contemplate his ravages. Amongst all the shocking and all the terrible scenes the world exhibits, the slaughters of war stand preeminent; yet these are the scenes of which the compassionate and the ferocious, the good and the bad, alike talk with complacency or exultation.

===Chinese===

罗伯逊（Robertson）曾说：「另一时代的恶行令我们震惊与骇然；我们自己时代的恶行却变得习以为常，几乎不引起惊恐。」——「任何国民习俗，无论对理智还是对心灵的影响，以及它在曲解或泯灭至关重要的道德原则方面所能达到的程度，都是令人瞩目的。那些在1566年有闲暇反思和评判的人，对暗杀罪行的震惊程度，似乎并不亚于那些在激愤与冲动中犯下暗杀罪的人。」3

在日常生活中，很少有事情比战争对人道道德情感的摧残与败坏更为惊人。一个在公路上残杀旅人的人，会激起普遍的惊恐与憎恶；但那些听闻公路上被杀一人便心生恐怖的人，对于战场上千人被戮却漠然置之。那些一见到单具尸体便惊骇不已的人，凝视着堆积如山、被人类双手残害的人类尸骸，却能保持冰冷的冷漠。若发生一起谋杀案，报纸会登载详细报道，夹杂着许多同情之词、惊骇之字，以及对凶手早日落网的切切期盼。而在接下来的段落中，主编或许便会告知我们，他正匆忙将第二版付印，以便抢先向公众通报刚刚发生的一场交战中「击毙敌军850人」的捷报。由于战争，心灵的自然冲动似乎被悬置了，仿佛每当我们凝视那鲜血淋漓的肆虐时，血腥的魔鬼便有权对我们的感知施下魔咒。在世间所展现的一切令人震惊与可怖的景象中，战争的屠杀尤为显目；然而，不论是慈悲者还是凶残者，良善者还是邪恶者，面对这些景象时，却无不怡然自得或欢呼雀跃。

===Original===

England is a land of benevolence, and to human misery she is, of all nations, the most prompt in the extension of relief. The immolations of the Hindus fill us with compassion or horror, and we are zealously laboring to prevent them. The sacrifices of life by our own criminal executions are the subject of our anxious commiseration, and we are strenuously endeavoring to diminish their number. We feel that the life of a Hindu or a malefactor is a serious thing, and that nothing but imperious necessity should induce us to destroy the one, or to permit the destruction of the other. Yet what are these sacrifices of life in comparison with the sacrifices of war? In the late campaign in Russia, there fell, during 173 days in succession, an average of 2,900 men per day. More than five hundred thousand human beings in less than six months! And most of these victims expired with peculiar intensity of suffering. “Thou that teachest another, teachest thou not thyself?” We are carrying our benevolence to the Indies, but what becomes of it in Russia or at Leipzig? We are laboring to save a few lives from the gallows, but where is our solicitude to save them on the field? Life is life, wheresoever it be sacrificed, and has everywhere equal claims to our regard. I am not now inquiring whether war is right, but whether we do not regard its calamities with an indifference with which we regard no others, and whether that indifference does not make us acquiesce in evils and in miseries which we should otherwise prevent or condemn.

===Chinese===

英国是一个仁善之邦，面对人类的苦难，在所有国家中她是伸出援手最迅速的。印度人的自焚献祭让我们满怀同情或惊恐，我们正热切地致力于加以阻止。我们自身刑事处决所剥夺的生命，是我们焦虑同情的对象，我们正竭力减少其数量。我们感到，一个印度人或一个罪犯的生命是非同小可的，唯有迫不得已的绝对必要性，才能促使我们摧毁前者，或允许毁灭后者。然而，与战争所牺牲的生命相比，这些牺牲又算得了什么？在最近的俄国战役中，连续173天里，平均每天倒下2900人。不到六个月的时间里，超过五十万人类生灵丧生！而这些受害者绝大多数是在极度痛苦中死去的。「你既教导别人，还不教导自己吗？」我们将仁善之举推广至印度，但在俄国或莱比锡，这种仁善又何在？我们竭力从绞刑架下挽救几条生命，但在战场上，我们挽救生命的关切又在何处？生命无论在何处被牺牲，都是生命，在任何地方对我们的关照都有着平等的权利。我现在并非探究战争是否正当，而是探究我们看待战争的灾难时，是否带着对待其他灾害所未曾有过的冷漠，以及这种冷漠是否促使我们默认了若非如此我们本该制止或谴责的罪恶与苦难。

===Original===

Amongst the immediate causes of the frequency of war, there is one which is, indisputably, irreconcilable in its nature with the principles of our religion. I speak of the critical sense of national pride, and consequent aptitude of offence, and violence of resentment. National irritability is at once a cause of war, and an effect. It disposes us to resent injuries with bloodshed and destruction; and a war, when it is begun, inflames and perpetuates the passions that produced it. Those who wish a war, endeavor to rouse the spirit of a people by stimulating their passions. They talk of the insult, or the encroachments, or the contempts of the destined enemy, with every artifice of aggravation; they tell us of foreigners who want to trample upon our rights, of rivals who ridicule our power, of foes who will crush, and of tyrants who will enslave us. These men pursue their object, certainly, by efficacious means; they desire a war, and therefore irritate our passions, knowing that when men are angry they are easily persuaded to fight.

In this state of irritability, a nation is continually alive to occasions of offence; and when we seek for offenses, we readily find them. A jealous sensibility sees insults and injuries where sober eyes see nothing; and nations thus surround themselves with a sort of artificial tentacula, which they throw wide in quest of irritation, and by which they are stimulated to revenge, by every touch of accident or inadvertency.

He that is easily offended will also easily offend. The man who is always on the alert to discover trespasses on his honor or his rights, never fails to quarrel with his neighbors. Such a person may be dreaded as a torpedo. We may fear, but we shall not love him; and fear, without love, easily lapses into enmity. There are, therefore, many feuds and litigations in the life of such a man, that would never have disturbed its quiet, if he had not captiously snarled at the trespasses of accident, and savagely retaliated insignificant injuries. The viper that we chance to molest, we suffer to live if he continue to be quiet, but if he raise himself in menaces of destruction, we knock him on the head.

===Chinese===

在战争频繁发生的直接原因中，无可争辩地存在一个其本质与我们宗教的原则绝不相容的原因。我所指的，就是对民族自尊的过分敏感，以及由此产生的极易被冒犯的倾向和强烈的怨恨情绪。民族易怒性既是战争的原因，也是其结果。它使我们倾向于用流血和毁灭来报复所受的伤害；而一场战争一旦打响，又会煽动并延续产生它的那些狂热激情。那些渴望战争的人，企图通过刺激公众的激情来唤起民众的斗志。他们极尽夸大之能事，大谈假想敌的侮辱、侵犯或藐视；他们告诉我们，外邦人想要践踏我们的权利，对手在嘲笑我们的力量，仇敌企图击垮我们，暴君企图奴役我们。这些人确实是以行之有效的手段来追求其目标的；他们渴望战争，因而激化我们的激情，因为他们深知，当人们被激怒时，最容易被说服去投入战斗。

在这一易怒状态下，一个国家对受冒犯的机会时刻保持警觉；当我们主动寻找冒犯时，往往不费吹灰之力就能找到。嫉妒的敏感在清醒之人眼中空无一物之处看到了侮辱与伤害；各民族因而让自己被一层人工触须所包围，四处伸展以搜寻激怒的信号，只要受到意外或疏忽的微小触动，便被激发起复仇的冲动。

容易被冒犯的人，往往也容易冒犯他人。一个时刻警惕着寻找侵犯其荣誉或权利的人，必然总会与邻人发生冲突。这样的人就像电鳐（torpedo）一样令人畏惧。我们或许会害怕他，但绝不会爱他；而没有爱的恐惧，极易蜕变为敌意。因此，在此类人的一生中，存在着诸多宿怨与纷争，如果不是他挑剔地对意外过失横加咆哮、对微不足道的伤害施以野蛮报复，其生活本不会受到任何打扰。我们偶然触碰到的毒蛇，若它保持安静，我们尚且容它活命；但若它直起身子摆出毁灭的威胁，我们便会迎头给它一击。

===Original===

It is with nations as with men. If, on every offence we fly to arms, and raise the cry of blood, we shall of necessity provoke exasperation; and if we exasperate a people as petulant and bloody as ourselves, we may probably continue to butcher one another, until we cease only from emptiness of exchequers, or weariness of slaughter. To threaten war, is therefore often equivalent to beginning it. In the present state of man’s principles, it is not probable that one nation will observe another levying men, and building ships and founding cannon, without providing men and ships and cannon themselves; and when both are thus threatening and defying, what is the hope that there will not be a war?

It will scarcely be disputed that we should not kill one another unless we cannot help it. Since war is an enormous evil, some sacrifices are expedient for the sake of peace; and if we consulted our understandings more and our passions less, we should soberly balance the probabilities of mischief, and inquire whether it be not better to endure some evils that we can estimate, than to engage in a conflict of which we can neither calculate the mischief, nor foresee the event; which may probably conduct us from slaughter to disgrace, and which at last is determined, not by justice, but by power. Pride may declaim against these sentiments; but my business is not with *pride*, but with *reason*; and I think reason determines that it would be more wise, and religion that it would be less wicked, to diminish our punctiliousness and irritability. If nations fought only when they could not be at peace, there would be very little fighting in the world. The wars that are waged for “insults to flags,” and an endless train of similar motives, are perhaps generally attributable to the irritability of our pride. We are at no pains to appear pacific towards the offender; our remonstrance is a threat; and the nation, which would give satisfaction to an *inquiry*, will give no other answer to a menace than a menace in return. At length we begin to fight, not because we are aggrieved, but because we are angry.

===Chinese===

国家之间亦如人与人之间。倘若每次受到冒犯我们都诉诸武力、高呼流血，我们必然会激起民愤；而如果我们激怒了一个与我们同样暴躁、残暴的民族，我们很可能会继续自相残杀，直到国库耗尽，或因屠杀的疲倦而不得不停手。因此，威胁战争往往等同于发动战争。在人类当下的原则状态下，一个国家若目睹另一国招募士兵、建造舰船、铸造大炮，自己不大可能不筹备军队、战舰和大炮；而当双方都如此互相威胁、彼此挑战时，又何谈不爆发战争的希望呢？

只要并非迫不得已，我们不应自相残杀，这一点恐难有争议。既然战争是巨大的恶，为了和平做出一些牺牲便是明智的；如果我们多运用理智，少凭任激情，我们就会冷静权衡祸害的几率，探究忍受一些我们能够估量之祸患，是否强过投入一场我们既无法测算其灾祸、又无法预知其结局的冲突——这场冲突很可能将我们从屠杀引向耻辱，且归根结底并非由正义决定，而是由强权决定。骄傲可能会驳斥这些观点；然而我的职责不是探讨骄傲，而是探讨理性；我认为理性断定降低我们的过分挑剔与易怒会更为明智，而宗教断定这样做会减少邪恶。如果各国唯有在无法维持和平时才开战，世间的战事就会微乎其微。那些因「侮辱国旗」以及一整串类似动机而发动的战争，大体上通常可归因于我们骄傲的易怒性。我们毫不费心向冒犯者展现和平姿态；我们的抗议本身就是威胁；而一个本可对质询（inquiry）给予满意答复的国家，面对威胁除了以威胁作为回敬，绝不会给出别的答复。到头来我们开战，并非因为我们受了委屈，而是因为我们被激怒了。

===Original===

The object of the haughtiness and petulance which one nation uses towards another, is of course to produce some benefit; to awe into compliance with its demands, or into forbearance from aggression. Now it ought to be distinctly shown, that petulance and haughtiness are more efficacious than calmness and moderation; that an address to the passions of a probable enemy is more likely to avert mischief from ourselves, than an address to their reason and their virtue.

Nations are composed of men, and of men with human feelings. Whether with individuals or with communities, “a soft answer turneth away wrath.” There is, indeed, something in the calmness of reason﻿—in an endeavor to convince rather than to intimidate﻿—in an honest solicitude for friendliness and peace, which obtains, which commands, which extorts forbearance and esteem. This is the privilege of rectitude and truth. It is an inherent quality of their nature: an evidence of their identity with perfect wisdom. I believe, therefore, that even as it concerns our *interests*, moderation and forbearance would be the most politic. And let not our *duties* be forgotten; for forbearance and moderation are duties, absolutely and indispensably imposed upon us by Jesus Christ.

The “balance of power” is a phrase with which we are made sufficiently familiar, as one of the great objects of national policy, that must be attained, at whatever cost of treasure or of blood. The support of this balance, therefore, is one of the great purposes of war, and one of the great occasions of its frequency.

===Chinese===

一国对另一国所施展的傲慢与乖戾，其目的自然是为了谋求某种利益；旨在慑服对方顺从其要求，或阻止对方发动侵略。现在理应清楚地证明，乖戾与傲慢是否比冷静与克制更为有效；诉诸潜在敌人的激情，是否比诉诸其理智与美德更有可能使我们自身免受祸害。

国家是由人组成的，由具备人类情感的人组成。不论是对个人还是对群体，「温和的回答使忿怒平息」。在理性的沉静中——在力图说服而非恐吓的努力中——在对友好与和平的真诚关切中，确实存在着某种力量，能够赢得、支配并博得忍让与敬重。这是正直与真理的特权。这是它们本性中固有的品质：是它们与完美智慧同一的明证。因此我相信，即使就我们的利益（interests）而言，克制与宽容也是最具远见的。切莫忘记我们的责任（duties）；因为忍耐与克制，是耶稣基督（Jesus Christ）绝对且不可推卸地加于我们的责任。

「势力均衡」（balance of power）是我们耳熟能详的一个短语，被视为国家政策的伟大目标之一，无论耗费多少财富与鲜血都必须达到。因此，维系这种平衡便成了战争的重大目的之一，也是战争频发的一大缘由。

===Original===

It is, perhaps, not idle to remark, that a balance of power amongst nations, is inherently subject to continual interruption. If all the countries of Europe were placed on an equality today, they would of necessity become unequal tomorrow. This is the inevitable tendency of human affairs. Thousands of circumstances which sagacity cannot foresee, will continually operate to destroy an equilibrium. Of men, who enter the world with the same possessions and the same prospects, one becomes rich and the other poor; one harangues in the Senate, and another labors in a mine; one sacrifices his life to intemperance, and another starves in a garret. How accurately soever we may adjust the strength and consequence of nations to each other, the failure of one harvest, the ravages of one tempest, the ambition of one man, may unequalize them in a moment. It is, therefore, not a trifling argument against this anxious endeavor to attain an equipoise of power, to find that no equipoise can be maintained. When negotiation has followed negotiation, and treaty has been piled upon treaty, and war has succeeded to war, the genius of a Napoleon, or the fate of an armada, nullifies our labors without the possibility of prevention. I do not know how much nations have gained by a balance of power, but it is worth remembrance that some of those countries which have been most solicitous to preserve it, have been most frequently fighting with each other. How many wars has a balance of power prevented, in comparison with the number that have been waged to maintain it?

It is, indeed, deplorable enough that such a balance is to be desired; and that the wickedness and violence of mankind are so great, that nothing can prevent them from destroying one another, but an equality of the means of destruction. In such a state of malignity and outrage, it need not be disputed, that, if it could be maintained, an equality of strength is sufficiently desirable; as tigers may be restrained from tearing one another by mutual fear, without any want of savageness. It should be remembered, then, that whatever can be said in favor of a balance of power, can be said only because we are wicked; that it derives all its value from our crimes; and that it is wanted only to restrain the outrage of our violence, and to make us contented to growl when we should otherwise fight.

===Chinese===

指出国家间的势力均衡天生必然会遭受不断的中断，这或许并非空话。倘若今日欧洲所有国家都处于均等地位，明日它们也必然会变得不均等。这是人类事务不可避免的趋势。明智之人无法预见的成千上万种情况，将不断打破这种均衡。在带着同样财富与前景踏入尘世的人当中，一人富足，另一人贫困；一人在参议院慷慨陈词，另一人在矿井辛勤劳作；一人因放纵无度丧命，另一人在阁楼忍饥挨饿。无论我们如何精确地调节各国之间的实力与重要性，一场歉收、一场风暴的摧残、一个人的野心，都能在刹那间使其失去均势。因此，发现根本没有任何均势可以维持，本身就构成了反对这种追求实力均势之焦虑努力的有力论据。当谈判接着谈判，条约堆叠在条约之上，战争接替了战争，拿破仑的天才，或一支舰队的宿命，便会让我们一切防范的努力化为泡影。我不知道各国通过势力均衡获得了多少好处，但值得铭记的是，那些最热衷于维持均势的国家，往往是彼此交战最频繁的国家。与为了维持势力均衡而发动的战争数量相比，它究竟阻止了多少场战争？

必须寄希望于这种平衡，人类的邪恶与暴力如此巨大，以至于除毁灭手段的对等之外别无他物能阻止他们自相残杀，这委实令人扼腕叹息。在这样一种充满恶意与暴虐的状态下，毋庸争辩，若能维持，实力的对等确实是十分令人向往的；正如同老虎之间出于相互戒惧可能克制不相互撕咬，而丝毫未减其凶残一般。因此应当谨记，凡赞成势力均衡的言论，唯有在我们邪恶的前提下才能成立；它的全部价值皆源于我们的罪愆；它的存在只是为了遏制我们暴行的肆虐，让我们在原本会大打出手时满足于龇牙低吼而已。

===Original===

Wars are often promoted from considerations of interest, as well as from passion. The love of gain adds its influence to our other motives to support them, and without other motives, we know that this love is sufficient to give great obliquity to the moral judgment, and to tempt us to many crimes. During a war of ten years, there will always be many whose income depends on its continuance; and a countless host of commissaries, and purveyors, and agents, and mechanics, commend a war, because it fills their pockets. These men have commonly but one question respecting a war, and that is﻿—whether they get by it. This is the standard of their decision, and this regulates the measure of their support. If money is in prospect, the desolation of a kingdom is of little concern; destruction and slaughter are not to be put in competition with a hundred a year. In truth, it seems to be the system of the conductors of a war, to give to the sources of gain every possible ramification. The more there are who profit by it, the more numerous will be its supporters; and thus the wishes of the cabinet become united with the avarice of the people, and both are gratified in slaughter and devastation.

A support more systematic and powerful is, however, given to war, because it offers to the higher ranks of society, a profession which unites gentility with profit, and which, without the *vulgarity* of trade, maintains or enriches them. It is of little consequence to inquire whether the distinction of vulgarity between the toils of war and the toils of commerce, be fictitious. In the abstract, it is fictitious; but of this species of reputation public opinion holds the *arbitrium, et jus, et norma*﻿—and public opinion is in favor of war.

===Chinese===

战争往往既因利益考量而受到鼓动，亦因激情而受到推动。对利益的贪图与我们支持战争的其他动机交织在一起；即便没有其他动机，我们也深知这种贪图足以对道德判断造成巨大的扭曲，诱使我们犯下诸多罪行。在一场持续十年的战争中，总会有许多人依赖战争的延续获取收入；无数的军需官、军饷采购商、代理人与机工工匠极力称赞战争，因为战争充实了他们的腰包。这些人对待战争通常只有一个疑问，那就是——他们能否从中获利。这是他们决策的准绳，也决定了他们支持战争的力度。如果有金钱前景，一个王国的荒芜他们微不足道；毁灭与屠杀在每年一百镑的收益面前根本不值一提。事实上，战争的操弄者似乎有一套机制，尽可能将谋利的渠道延伸到各个枝节。从中获利的人越多，战争的支持者就越众；于是内阁的意愿与民众的贪欲合流，二者在屠杀与蹂躏中皆大欢喜。

然而，战争之所以获得更为系统和强有力的支持，是因为它为社会的较高阶层提供了一种集高雅与收益于一身的职业，这种职业免去了经商的「粗俗（vulgarity）」，能够维持他们的地位或使他们致富。探究战争劳作与商业劳作之间粗俗与否的界分是否虚妄，并无多大意义。就抽象而言，这确是虚妄的；但对这种名誉，公众舆论握有「裁决权、法律与规范（arbitrium, et jus, et norma）」——而公众舆论是偏向于战争的。

===Original===

The army and the navy therefore afford to the middle and higher classes, a most acceptable profession. The profession of arms is like the profession of law or physic﻿—a regular source of employment and profit. Boys are educated for the army, as they are educated for the bar; and parents appear to have no other idea than that war is part of the business of the world. Of *younger sons*, whose fathers do not choose to support them at the expense of the heir, the army and the navy are the common resource. They would not know what to do without them. To many of these, the news of a peace becomes a calamity; principle is not powerful enough to cope with interest; they prefer the desolation of the world to the loss of a colonelcy. It is in this manner that much of the rank, the influence, and the wealth of a country become interested in the promotion of wars; and when a custom is promoted by wealth, and influence, and rank, what is the wonder that it should be continued?

Yet it is a dreadful consideration that the destruction of our fellows should become a business by which to live; and that a man can find no other occupation of gain, than that of butchering his neighbors. It is said (if my memory serves me, by Sir Walter Raleigh), “He that taketh up his rest to live by this profession shall hardly be an honest man.” “Where there is *no obligation to obey*,” says Lord Clarendon, “it is a wonderful, and an unnatural appetite, that disposes men to be soldiers, that they may know how to live; and what reputation soever it may have in politics, it can have none in religion, to say, that the art and conduct of a soldier is not infused by nature, but by study, experience and observation; and therefore that men are to *learn it*:﻿—when, in truth, *this common argument is made by appetite to excuse, and not by reason to support*, an ill custom.”4 People do not often become soldiers in order to serve their country, but to serve themselves. An income is commonly the motive to the great, and idleness to the poor. To plead the love of our country is therefore hypocrisy; and let it be remembered that hypocrisy is itself an evidence, and an acknowledgment that the motive which it would disguise is bad.

===Chinese===

因此，陆军与海军为中产阶级和较高阶层提供了一种极受欢迎的职业。从军就如从事法律或医术一样——是一项常规的就业与获利来源。男孩子接受教育去从军，正如他们接受教育去成为律师；父母们似乎除认为战争是世间事务的一部分之外，别无其他观念。对于那些父亲不愿以牺牲继承人财产为代价来资助的次子们，陆军和海军是常见的退路。若没有这两条出路，他们将不知如何自处。对他们当中的许多人而言，和平的消息简直就是一场灾难；道德原则不足以与利益相抗衡；比起失去陆军上校（colonelcy）的职位，他们更宁愿让整个世界陷于荒芜。正是通过这种方式，一个国家的诸多显贵地位、影响力和财富卷入了推动战争的利益链条之中；而当一种习俗受到财富、影响力和地位的共同推动时，它得以长存又有何可奇怪的呢？

然而，残害我们的同胞竟然成为借以谋生的营生，一个人除了屠戮他的邻人之外竟找不到其他获取利益的职业，这委实是一种可怕的考量。正如沃尔特·罗利爵士（Sir Walter Raleigh，若我未记错的话）所言：「凡决意以此行业谋生者，断难成为一个诚实正直之人。」克拉伦登勋爵（Lord Clarendon）亦云：「在毫无服从义务之处，促使人们为了谋生而去当兵，是一种异乎寻常且反常的欲望；无论这在政治上拥有何种声誉，在宗教上却毫无声誉可言，说什么士兵的技艺与行为并非由天性赋予，而是源于钻研、经验与观察，因此人们必须去学习它：——而在事实上，这一通行论点不过是欲望用来为一种恶习开脱的借口，而非理性用来支撑它的依据。」4 人们去从军，往往并非为了效忠国家，而是为了谋求私利。对于权贵而言，动机通常是薪俸；对于穷人而言，动机通常是逃避劳役游手好闲。因此，以热爱祖国为借口纯属伪善；须谨记，伪善本身就是一种证据和承认，表明它所企图掩饰的动机是不正当的。

===Original===

By depending upon war for a subsistence, a powerful inducement is given to desire it; and I would submit it to the conscientious part of the profession, that he who desires a war for the sake of its profits has lost something of his virtue; he has, at least enlisted one of the most influential of human propensities against it, and when the prospect of gratification is before him﻿—when the question of war is to be decided﻿—it is to be feared that he will suffer the whispers of interest to prevail, and that humanity and religion, and his conscience will be sacrificed to promote it. But whenever we shall have learned the nature of pure Christianity, and have imbibed its dispositions, we shall not be willing to avail ourselves of such a horrible source of profit; nor to contribute to the misery, and wickedness, and destruction of mankind, in order to avoid a false and foolish shame.

===Chinese===

由于依赖战争谋生，人们便产生了一种渴望战争的强烈诱因；我谨向这一职业中有良知的部分人进言：凡为了战争的利益而渴望战争之人，已经丧失了其部分美德；他至少已经动员了人类最具影响力的一种倾向来反对美德，而当满足欲望的前景摆在他面前——当开战的问题有待裁决时——令人担忧的是，他将任由利益的窃窃私语占据上风，而人性、宗教和他的良知都将被牺牲，以推动战争。然而，只要我们领悟了纯正基督教（pure Christianity）的本质，吸纳了它的秉性，我们就绝不会愿意利用这样一种可怕的获利来源，也绝不会为了避免虚妄而愚蠢的羞耻感，去助长人类的苦难、邪恶与毁灭。

===Original===

It is frequently in the power of individual statesmen to involve a people in a war. “Their restraints,” says Knox, “in the pursuit of political objects, are not those of morality and religion, but solely reasons of state and political caution. Plausible words are used, but they are used to hide the deformity of the real principles. Whenever war is deemed desirable in an interested view, a specious pretext never yet remained unfound;”5﻿—and “when they have once said what they think convenient, how untruly soever, they proceed to do what they judge will be profitable, how unjustly soever; and this men very absurdly and unreasonably would have called *reason of state*, to the discredit of all solid reason, and all rules of probity.”6 Statesmen have two standards of morality﻿—a social and a political standard. Political morality embraces all crimes; except, indeed, that it has that technical virtue which requires that he who may kill a hundred men with bullets, should not kill one with arsenic. And from this double system of morals it happens, that statesmen who have no restraint to political enormities but political expediency, are sufficiently amiable in private life. But “probity,” says Bishop Watson, “is an uniform principle; it cannot be put on in our private closet, and put off in the council-chamber or the senate;” and I fear that he who is wicked as a statesman, if he be good as a man, has some other motive to goodness than its love: that he is decent in private life, because it is not *expedient* that he should be flagitious. It cannot be hoped that he has much restraint from principle. I believe, however, the time will come, when it will be found that God has instituted but one standard of morality, and that to that standard is required the universal conformity of nations, and of men.

Of the wars of statesmen’s ambition, it is not necessary to speak, because no one to whom the world will listen, is willing to defend them.

===Chinese===

个别政治家往往有权将一个民族卷入战争。「他们在追求政治目标时的制约，」诺克斯（Knox）说，「并非道德与宗教的制约，而纯粹是国家理由（reasons of state）与政治谨慎。看似冠冕堂皇的说辞被用来掩盖真实原则的丑陋。每当出于私利考量而认为战争有利时，一个堂而皇之的借口向来就不难找到；」5——而且「他们一旦说了他们认为合宜的话，不论何等不实，便着手去做他们判定有利可图的事，不论何等不义；而人们却荒谬绝伦、毫无道理地将此称为『国家理由』，从而败坏了一切扎实的理性与一切正直的准则。」6 政治家拥有两套道德标准——一套社交标准，一套政治标准。政治道德包罗了一切罪行；诚然，除了一种技术性的美德，即要求那个能用子弹射杀百人的人，不应使用砒霜去毒杀一人。由于这种双重道德体系的存在，那些除了政治权宜之计外对政治暴行毫无约束的政治家，在私人生活中往往颇为和蔼可亲。然而，「正直（probity），」沃森主教（Bishop Watson）说，「是一贯的原则；它不能在我们的私人密室中穿上，而在会议厅或参议院中脱下；」而我担心，一个身为政治家时邪恶、身为凡人时良善的人，其向善的动机并非出于对善的热爱：他在私人生活中端庄得体，仅仅是因为作恶并不合算（not expedient）。指望他受到原则的多大约束是不可企及的。然而我相信，终有一日人们会发现，上帝只设立了一条道德标准，而这条标准要求各国与万民普遍遵行。

至于政治家出于野心而发动的战争，则无需赘言，因为世人愿意倾听的人当中，无人愿为此类战争辩护。

===Original===

But statesmen have, besides ambition, many purposes of nice policy which make wars convenient; and when they have such purposes, they are cool speculators in blood. They who have many dependents have much patronage, and they who have much patronage have much power. By a war, thousands become dependent on a minister; and if he be disposed, he can often pursue schemes of guilt, and entrench himself in unpunished wickedness, because the wear enables him to silence the clamor of opposition by an office, and to secure the suffrages of venality by a bribe. He has therefore, many motives to war, in ambition that does not refer to conquest; or, in fear, that extends only to his office or his pocket: and fear or ambition are sometimes more interesting considerations than the happiness and the lives of men. Or perhaps he wants to immortalize his name by a splendid administration; and he thinks no splendor so great as that of conquest and plunder. Cabinets have, in truth, many secret motives of wars of which the people know little. They talk in public of invasions of right, of breaches of treaty, of the support of honor, of the necessity of retaliation, when these motives have no influence on their determination. Some untold purpose of expediency, or the private quarrel of a prince, or the pique or anger of a minister, are often the real motives to a contest, whilst its promoters are loudly talking of the honor or the safety of the country. The motives to war are indeed without end to their number, or their iniquity, or their insignificance. What was the motive of Xerxes in his invasion of Greece?

It is to be feared that the world has sometimes seen the example of a war, begun and prosecuted for the simple purpose of appeasing the clamors of a people by diverting their attention:

“I well might lodge a fear

To be again displaced; which, to avoid

I cut them off, and had a purpose now

To lead out many to the Holy Land,

Lest rest and lying still might make them look

Too near into my state. Therefore, my Harry,

Be it thy course to busy giddy minds

With foreign quarrels; that action hence borne out

May waste the memory of former days.”

===Chinese===

然而，政治家除了野心之外，还有许多微妙的政治盘算使得战争变得便利；当他们怀有此类目的时，他们便成了冷酷的鲜血投机者。门客众多者权力庇荫亦广，权力庇荫广者权势亦大。通过一场战争，成千上万的人变得依附于大臣；只要大臣有意，他往往能推行罪恶的阴谋，并在不受惩罚的邪恶中自我巩固，因为战争使他能够以官职平息反对派的喧嚣，并以贿赂收买贪婪者的选票。因此，除了与征服无关的野心之外，他还有许多发动战争的动机；或者出于仅关乎其官职或钱袋的恐惧：而恐惧或野心，有时比人类的幸福与生命是更有吸引力的考量。或者，也许他希望通过辉煌的施政使自己的名字流芳百世；而他认为没有任何光彩能比得上征服与掠夺的光彩。内阁事实上怀有许多民间知之甚少的秘密战争动机。他们在公开场合高谈权利受到侵犯、条约遭到背弃、维护国家荣誉、报复的必要性，而这些动机对其决定毫无影响。某种不可告人的权宜目的、某位君主的私人嫌隙、某位大臣的怨气或愤怒，往往才是一场冲突的真正动机，而其推动者却在歇斯底里地高谈国家的荣誉或安全。战争的动机不论就其数量、罪恶还是微不足道而言，确实无穷无尽。薛西斯（Xerxes）入侵希腊的动机究竟是什么？

令人担忧的是，世间有时目睹过这样一种战争的例证，其发动与推进仅仅是为了通过转移公众视线来平息民众的喧嚣：

「我大可心存担忧
唯恐再次被废黜；为免遭此劫，
我除掉了他们，如今更怀抱意图
率众远征圣地，
免得赋闲安坐使他们
过于细察我的国政。因此，我的哈里，
愿你的方略是用外邦战事
让浮躁的心思疲于奔命；如此兴师动众
方可消磨前朝往事的记忆。」

===Original===

When the profligacy of a minister, or the unpopularity of his measures, has excited public discontent, he can perhaps find no other way of escaping the resentment of the people, than by thus making them forget it. He therefore discovers a pretext for denouncing war on some convenient country, in order to divert the indignation of the public from himself to their new-made enemies. Such wickedness has existed, and may exist again. Surely it is nearly the climax of possible iniquity. I know not whether the records of human infamy present another crime of such enormous or such abandoned wickedness. A monstrous profligacy or ferocity that must be, which for the sole purpose of individual interest, enters its closet, and coolly fabricates pretences for slaughter; that quietly contrives the exasperation of the public hatred, and then flings the lighted brands of war amongst the devoted and startling people.

The public, therefore, whenever a war is designed, should diligently inquire into the motives of engaging in it. It should be an inquiry that will not be satisfied with idle declamations on indeterminate dangers, and that is not willing to take anything upon trust. The public should see the danger for themselves; and if they do not see it, should refuse to be led, blindfold, to murder their neighbors. This, we think, is the public duty, as it is certainly the public interest. It implies a forgetfulness of the ends and purposes of government, and of the just degrees and limitations of obedience, to be hurried into so dreadful a measure as a war, without knowing the reason, or asking it. A people have the power of prevention, and they ought to exercise it. Let me not, however, be charged with recommending violence or resistance. The power of preventing war consists in the power of *refusing to take part in it*. This, is the mode of opposing political evil, which Christianity permits, and, in truth, requires. And as it is the most Christian method, so, as it respects war, it were certainly the most efficacious; for it is obvious that war cannot be carried on without the cooperation of the people.

===Chinese===

当一位大臣的放荡不羁或其法令的不得民心激起了公愤时，他或许除了以此种方式让人们忘却怨恨之外，别无其他逃脱民愤的途径。因此，他便寻得向某个方便的国家宣战的借口，以便将公众的愤怒从自己身上转移到新树立的敌人身上。这种邪恶曾经存在过，且可能再次发生。这无疑几乎达到了可能之罪恶的顶峰。我不知道人类耻辱的记录中是否还记载着另一桩如此滔天、如此肆无忌惮的邪恶罪行。那该是何等骇人听闻的放荡或残暴，纯粹为了个人利益，便步入密室，冷酷地编造屠杀的借口；悄然策划激化公众的仇恨，继而将战争的燃烧火把抛向那些注定受难且惊惶失措的人民之中。

因此，每当谋划一场战争时，公众理应勤勉地探究卷入战争的动机。这种探究不应满足于对虚幻危险的空洞陈词，也不应盲目听信任何说法。公众应当亲自认清危险；若未看清，就应拒绝被蒙蔽双眼去残杀邻邦。我们认为，这是公众的职责，也必然符合公众的利益。在不知缘由、亦不探问原委的情况下匆忙卷入战争这一可怕的举措，意味着忘却了政府的宗旨与目的，忘却了服从的正当限度与边界。人民拥有阻止战争的权力，且理应行使这一权力。然而，切莫指责我是在鼓吹暴力或抵抗。阻止战争的权力，在于拒绝参与战争的权力。这正是基督教所允许、且事实上所要求的对抗政治罪恶的方式。正如这是最符合基督之道的方法一样，就战争而言，这无疑也是最行之有效的方法；因为显而易见，没有人民的协同合作，战争便无法进行。

===Original===

But I believe the greatest cause of the popularity of war, and of the facility with which we engage in it, consists in this: that an idea of glory is attached to military exploits, and of honor to the military profession. Something of elevation is supposed to belong to the character of the soldier; whether it be that we involuntarily presume his personal courage; or that he who makes it his business to defend the rest of the community, acquires the superiority of a protector; or that the profession implies an exemption from the laborious and the “meaner” occupations of life. There is something in war, whether phantom or reality, which glitters and allures; and the allurement is powerful, since we see that it induces us to endure hardships and injuries, and expose life to a continual danger. Men do not become soldiers because life is indifferent to them, but because of some extrinsic circumstances which attach to the profession; and some of the most influential of these circumstances are the fame, the spirit, the honor, the glory, which mankind agree to belong to the warrior. The glories of battle, and of those who perish in it, or who return in triumph to their country, are favorite topics of declamation with the historian, the biographer, and the poet. They have told us a thousand times of *dying heroes*, who “resign their lives amidst the joys of conquest, and filled with England’s glory, smile in death;” and thus every excitement that eloquence and genius can command is employed to arouse that ambition of fame which can be gratified only at the expense of blood.

There are many ways in which a soldier derives pleasure from his profession. A military officer,7 when he walks the street, is an object of *notice*; he is a man of *spirit*, of *honor*, of *gallantry*; wherever he be, he is distinguished from ordinary men; he is an acknowledged *gentleman*. If he engage in battle, he is *brave*, and *noble*, and *magnanimous*: If he be killed, he has *died for his country*; he has *closed his career with glory*. Now, all this is agreeable to the mind; it flatters some of its strongest and most pervading passions; and the gratification which these passions derive from war, is one of the great reasons why men so willingly engage in it.

===Chinese===

但我相信，战争之所以受到欢迎以及我们之所以如此轻易卷入战争的最大原因，在于这一点：人们将荣耀的观念赋予了军事功勋，将尊荣的观念赋予了军旅职业。某种崇高感被认为属于军人的品格；不论是因为我们不由自主地推崇他的个人勇气；还是因为那个以保卫社群其余成员为己任的人，获得了保护者的优越地位；亦或是因为这一职业意味着免于从事生活中繁重而「低微」的行当。在战争中，不论是幻影还是现实，总有某种闪烁着诱人光芒的东西；这种诱惑是强有力的，因为我们看到它促使人们忍受艰难与伤害，将生命置于持续的危险之中。人们成为士兵并非因为生命对他们无足轻重，而是因为依附于该职业的某些外在环境；而在这些环境中，最具影响力的便是全人类公认属于武士的名望、气魄、尊严与荣耀。战役的荣耀，以及战死沙场者或凯旋归国者的荣光，是历史学家、传记作家和诗人最喜爱的宣叙主题。他们成千上万次地向我们讲述那些垂死英雄（dying heroes），他们「在征服的喜悦中交出生命，满怀着英格兰的荣光，含笑而逝」；于是，凡雄辩与天才所能驾驭的一切激动人心的手段都被利用起来，用以激发那种唯有用鲜血为代价方能满足的名望野心。

一个士兵从其职业中获取快乐的方式有很多。一名军官，7 当他走在街上时，是一个引人注目的焦点；他是一个有气魄（spirit）、有荣誉感（honor）、有风度（gallantry）的人；不论身在何处，他都卓尔不群于常人；他被公认为是一位绅士（gentleman）。如果他投身战斗，他就是勇敢、高尚且宽宏大量的；如果他战死沙场，他就是为国捐躯；他以光荣结束了自己的生涯。这一切无不令人心旷神怡；它迎合了人心当中一些最强烈、最普遍的激情；而这些激情从战争中获得的满足，正是人们如此乐意投身战争的主要原因之一。

===Original===

Now we ask the question of a man of reason, what is the foundation of this fame and glory? We profess, that, according to the best of our powers of discovery, no solid foundation can be found. Upon the foundation, whatever it be, an immense structure is, however, raised﻿—a structure so vast, so brilliant, so attractive, that the greater portion of mankind are content to gaze in admiration, without any inquiry into its basis, or any solicitude for its durability. If, however, it should be, that the gorgeous temple will be able to stand only till Christian truth and light become predominant, it surely will be wise of those who seek a niche in its apartments as their paramount and final good, to pause ere they proceed. If they desire a reputation that shall outlive guilt and fiction, let them look to the basis of military fame. If this fame should one day sink into oblivion and contempt, it would not be the first instance in which widespread glory has been found to be a glittering bubble, that has burst, and been forgotten. Look at the days of chivalry! Of the ten thousand Quixotes of the middle ages, where is now the honor or the name? Yet poets once sang their praises, and the chronicler of their achievements believed he was recording an everlasting fame. Where are now the glories of the tournament? Glories

“Of which all Europe rang from side to side.”

Where is the champion whom princes caressed, and nobles envied? Where are now the triumphs of Duns Scotus, and where are the folios that perpetuated his fame? The glories of war have indeed outlived these. Human passions are less mutable than human follies; but I am willing to avow my conviction that these glories are alike destined to sink into forgetfulness; and that the time is approaching, when the applauses of heroism, and the splendors of conquest, will be remembered only as follies and iniquities that are past. Let him who seeks for fame, other than that which an era of Christian purity will allow, make haste; for every hour that he delays its acquisition will shorten its duration. This is certain, if there be certainty in the promises of heaven.

===Chinese===

现在我们向一个理性之人提出这个问题：这种名声与荣耀的根基究竟何在？我们坦言，依我们所能竭尽的发现能力而言，根本找不到任何坚实的根基。然而，无论基础为何，其上却矗立起一座宏伟的建筑——一座如此庞大、如此辉煌、如此诱人的建筑，以至于人类的大多数满足于赞叹凝望，既不探究其基石，亦不关切其持久性。然而，如果这座华丽的殿堂唯有等到基督的真理与光芒居于主导地位时方能立足，那么对于那些将在此殿堂中谋求一席之地视为首要与终极福祉的人来说，在继续前行之前先行驻足无疑是明智的。如果他们渴望一种能够超越罪愆与虚构的长存声誉，就让他们审视军事声誉的基石吧。如果这种名声有朝一日沉沦于遗忘与鄙弃之中，这绝非普遍荣耀被发现为破灭且遭遗忘的闪烁泡沫之首例。看看骑士时代吧！中世纪成千上万的堂吉诃德（Quixotes）们，如今其名誉与名号安在？然而诗人曾几何时歌唱他们的赞歌，其功勋的记录者曾坚信自己正在铭刻永恒的名声。比武大会的荣耀如今又在何方？那些

「响彻欧洲四方的荣耀。」

备受君王宠爱、令贵族艳羡的勇士如今安在？邓斯·司各脱（Duns Scotus）的胜利如今安在？那些使他流芳百世的厚重卷册如今又在何方？战争的荣耀确实比这些存续得更久。人类的激情比人类的愚蠢更为不易更迭；但我愿意坦陈我的确信，这些荣耀同样注定要沉入忘川；那个时代正在临近，届时英雄主义的掌声与征服的辉煌，将仅作为过去的愚行与罪恶被人们忆起。让那个寻求非基督纯洁时代所允许之名声的人赶快行动吧；因为他迟延获取名声的每一刻，都将缩短其维持的时间。如果有任何确定性存在于上天的应许之中，这一点便是确凿无疑的。

===Original===

In inquiring into the foundation of military glory, it will be borne in mind, that it is acknowledged by our adversaries, that this glory *is not recognized by Christianity*. No part of the heroic character, says one of the great advocates of war, is the subject of the “commendation or precepts, or example” of Christ; but the character and dispositions most opposite to the heroic are the subject of them all.8 This is a great concession; and it surely is the business of Christians, who are sincere in their profession, to doubt the purity of that “glory” and the rectitude of that “heroic character,” which it is acknowledged that their Great Instructor never in any shape countenanced, and often obliquely condemned.9

If it be attempted to define *why* glory is allotted to the soldier, we suppose that we shall be referred to his skill, or his bravery, or his patriotism.

Of *skill* it is not necessary to speak, since very few have the opportunity of displaying it. The business of the great majority is only obedience; and obedience of that sort which almost precludes the exercise of talent.

===Chinese===

在探究军事荣耀的基石时，务必牢记，我们的对手亦承认，这种荣耀并未得到基督教的认可。一位主要的战争辩护者指出，基督的「赞赏、训诫或榜样」绝无任何部分以英武品质（heroic character）为主题；相反，与英武截然对立的品质与秉性，才是一切赞赏、训诫与榜样的主题。8 这是一个巨大的让步；对于真诚坚守其信仰的基督徒而言，去怀疑那种据承认他们的伟大导师（Great Instructor）从未以任何形式予以赞同、且往往委婉谴责的「荣耀」之纯洁性与「英武品质」之正当性，无疑是其本分所在。9

如果试图界定为何将荣耀赋予士兵，我们想，人们会归因于他的技能、他的英勇，或是他的爱国心。

至于技能（skill），则无需多言，因为极少有人有机会施展技能。绝大多数人的本分仅仅是服从；而且是那种几乎排除了才干发挥的服从。

===Original===

The rational and immortal being, who raises the edifice of his fame on simple *bravery*, has chosen but an unworthy and a frail foundation. Separate bravery from motives and purposes, and what will remain but that which is possessed by a mastiff or a gamecock? All just, all rational, and we will venture to affirm, all permanent reputation, refers to the mind or to virtue; and what connection has animal power or animal hardihood with intellect or goodness? I do not decry *courage*. I know that He who was better acquainted than we are with the nature and worth of human actions, attached much value to courage; but he attached none to bravery. Courage He recommended by his precepts, and enforced by his example: bravery He never recommended at all. The wisdom of this distinction, and its accordancy with the principles of his religion, are plain. Bravery requires the existence of many of those dispositions which He disallowed. Animosity, resentment, the desire of retaliation, the disposition to injure and destroy﻿—all this is necessary to bravery; but all this is incompatible with Christianity. The courage which Christianity requires is to bravery what fortitude is to daring﻿—an effort of the mind rather than of the spirits. It is a calm, steady determinateness of purpose, that will not be diverted by solicitation, or awed by fear. “Behold, I go bound in the spirit unto Jerusalem, not knowing the things that shall befall me there, save that the Holy Ghost witnesseth in every city, saying, that bonds and afflictions abide me. *But none of these things move me; neither count I my life dear unto myself.*”10 What resemblance has bravery to courage like this? This courage is a virtue, and a virtue which it is difficult to acquire or to practice; and we have, therefore, heedlessly or ingeniously, transferred its praise to another quality, which is inferior in its nature, and easier to acquire, in order that we may obtain the reputation of virtue at a cheap rate. That simple bravery implies much merit, it will be difficult to show﻿—at least, if it be meritorious, we think it will not always be easy, in awarding the honors of a battle, to determine the preponderance of virtue between the soldier and the horse which carries him.

===Chinese===

把名誉的大厦建立在单纯的勇猛（bravery）之上的理性和不朽的存在，所选择的不过是一个不相称且脆弱的根基。若将勇猛与动机和目的分离开来，除了大型猛犬或斗鸡所拥有的属性之外，还能剩下什么？一切公正的、一切理性的、我们敢断言一切持久的声誉，皆关乎心灵或美德；而肉体的力量或动物性的顽强与智力或善良又有何关联？我并不贬低勇气（courage）。我知道，那位比我们更通晓人类行为之本质与价值的主，高度重视勇气；但他绝不看重勇猛。勇气是他以训诫所推荐、以榜样所践行的；而勇猛他从未予以推荐。这种区分的智慧，及其与他的宗教原则的一致性，是昭然若揭的。勇猛需要许多他所不容许的秉性存在。敌意、怨恨、报复的欲望、伤害与摧毁的意图——这一切皆为勇猛所必需；但这一切皆与基督教格格不入。基督教所要求的勇气之于勇猛，正如坚毅之于鲁莽——是心灵的力量，而非血气之勇。它是一种沉着、坚定不移的目的决心，既不会被恳求所动摇，也不会被恐惧所吓倒。「现在我往耶路撒冷去，心甚迫切，不知道在那里要遇见什么事；但知道圣灵在各城里向我指证，说有捆锁与患难等待我。但我都不介意，也不看自己的性命为宝贵。」10 勇猛与这样的勇气有何相似之处？这种勇气是一种美德，一种难以获得、难以践行的美德；因此，我们漫不经心或别出心裁地，将其赞誉转移到了另一种品质之上，后者本性低劣，更容易获得，以便我们能以廉价的代价换取美德的名声。单纯的勇猛蕴含诸多值得称颂之处，这一点恐难加以证明——至少，若说它值得赞赏，我们认为在授予战役荣誉时，在士兵与驮载他的战马之间评判哪一方更具美德优势，并不总是容易的。

===Original===

But *patriotism* is the great foundation of the soldier’s glory. Patriotism is the universal theme. To “fight nobly for our country,”﻿—to “fall, covered with glory, in our country’s cause;” to “sacrifice our lives for the liberties, and laws, and religion of our country”﻿—are phrases in the mouth of every man. What do they mean, and to whom do they apply?

We contend that to say generally of those who perish in war, that “they have died for their country,” is simply untrue; and for this simple reason, that they did not fight for it. To impugn the notion of ages, is perhaps a hardy task; but we wish to employ, not dogmatism, but argument: and we maintain that men have commonly no such purity of motive, that they have no such patriotism. What is the officer’s motive to entering the army? We appeal to himself. Is it not *that he may obtain an income*? And what is the motive of the private? Is it not *that he prefers a life of idleness to industry*, or that he had no wish but *the wish for change*? Having entered the army, what, again, is the soldier’s motive to fight? Is it not that fighting is a part of his business﻿—that it is one of the conditions of his servitude? We are not now saying that these motives are bad, but we are saying that they *are* the motives, and that patriotism is *not*. Of those who fall in battle, is there one in a hundred who even thinks of his country’s good? He thinks, perhaps, of its glory, and of the honor of his regiment, but for his country’s advantage or welfare, he has no care and no thought. He fights, because fighting is a matter of course to a soldier, or because his personal reputation is at stake, or because he is compelled to fight, or because he thinks nothing at all of the matter; but seldom, indeed, because he wishes to benefit his country. He fights in battle, as a horse draws in a carriage, because he is compelled to do it, or because he has done it before; but he seldom thinks more of his country’s good, than the same horse, if he were carrying corn to a granary would think he was providing for the comforts of his master.

===Chinese===

然而，爱国主义（patriotism）是军人荣耀的伟大基石。爱国主义是一个普遍的主题。「为国崇高奋战」——「在祖国的事业中裹挟荣光倒下」——「为我们祖国的自由、法律与宗教牺牲生命」——是每个人口中挂着的言辞。它们究竟意味着什么？又适用于何人？

我们坚决主张，泛泛而言战死沙场之人「为国捐躯」，纯属不实之词；理由很简单，因为他们并非为了祖国而战。反驳历代以来的既定观念或许是一项艰巨的任务；但我们希望运用的不是教条主义，而是论证：我们断言，人们通常根本没有如此纯洁的动机，根本没有这样的爱国情怀。军官参军的动机是什么？我们不妨诉诸他本人。难道不是为了获得一份收入吗？而列兵的动机又是什么？难道不是因为相比勤奋劳作，他更偏好游手好闲的生活，或者除了渴望改变之外别无所求？进入军队之后，士兵打仗的动机又是什么？难道不是因为战斗是他本职工作的一部分——是他役使从属条件之一吗？我们现在并不是说这些动机是邪恶的，而是说它们确实就是其动机所在，而绝非出于爱国主义。在阵亡的将士中，一百个人里难道有一个人哪怕想到过自己国家的福祉吗？他想到的，或许是国家的荣耀和他所在团的荣誉，但对于国家的利益或福祉，他既无关切，亦无思索。他之所以打仗，是因为对士兵而言打仗是理所当然的事，或者因为他的个人声誉悬于一线，或者因为他是被迫作战，或者因为他对这件事根本毫无所思；然而，真正出于造福祖国的愿望而战的情况，委实少之又少。他在战役中作战，正如一匹马拉着马车，是因为被迫如此，或者因为从前就这样做过；但比起同一匹运送谷物到粮仓的马去思考自己正在为主人提供舒适生活，他对自己国家福祉的思考绝不会多出一分。

===Original===

And, indeed, if the soldier speculated on his country’s good, he often cannot tell how it is affected by the quarrel. Nor is it to be expected of him that he should know this. When there is a rumor of a war, there is an endless diversity of opinions as to its expediency, and endless oppositions of conclusion, whether it will tend more to the good of the country, to prosecute or avoid it. If senators and statesmen cannot calculate the good or evil of a war﻿—if one promises advantages and another predicts ruin﻿—how is the soldier to decide? And without deciding and promoting the *good*, how is he to be patriotic? Nor will much be gained by saying, that questions of policy form no part of his business, and that he has no other duty than obedience; since this is to reduce his agency to the agency of a machine; and moreover, by this rule, his arms might be directed, indifferently, to the annoyance of another country, or to the oppression of his own. The truth is, that we give to the soldier that of which we are wont to be sufficiently sparing﻿—a *gratuitous* concession of merit. In ordinary life, an individual maintains his individual opinions, and pursues correspondent conduct, with the approbation of one set of men, and the censures of another. One party says, he is benefiting his country, and another maintains that he is ruining it. But the soldier, for whatever he fights, and whether really in promotion of his country’s good, or in opposition to it, is always a patriot, and is always secure of his praise. If the war is a national calamity, and was foreseen to be such, still he *fights for his country*. If his judgment has decided against the war, and against its justice or expediency, still he *fights for his country*. He is always virtuous. If he but uses a bayonet, he is always a patriot.

===Chinese===

而且，事实上，即使士兵深思其国家的福祉，他也往往无法说清这场纷争将如何影响这一福祉。期望他了解这一点也是不切实际的。当战事风声初起时，关于其是否合宜存在着无休无止的意见分歧，关于继续推进还是避免战事是否更有利于国家福祉，结论更是截然对立。如果参议员和政治家都无法估量一场战争的利弊——如果一方许诺种种利益而另一方预言毁灭——士兵又该如何抉择？而若不做出抉择并促进善行，他又何谈爱国？若说政策问题不属于他的职责范围、他除了服从别无其他义务，这也无济于事；因为这无异于将他的能动性贬低为机器的运转；不仅如此，依此规则，他的武器可以不分青红皂白地用于侵扰别国，亦可用于压迫本国同胞。事实真相是，我们给予了士兵我们在日常生活中向来十分吝啬的东西——一种毫无依据的功绩让步。在世俗生活中，一个人坚守其个人见解并采取相应行动，会受到一部分人的赞许，同时遭到另一部分人的谴责。一派人说他在造福国家，另一派人则坚称他在毁灭国家。然而，士兵无论为何而战，无论真正是在促进国家的福祉还是与之相悖，他始终被冠以爱国者的名号，始终稳获赞誉。如果这场战争是一场举国灾难，且早被预见如此，他依然是在「为国而战」。如果他的理智判定反对这场战争，反对其正义性或合宜性，他依然是在「为国而战」。他永远是道德高尚的。只要他挥动刺刀，他便始终是一位爱国者。

===Original===

*To sacrifice our lives for the liberties, and laws, and religion of our native land*, are undoubtedly high-sounding words:﻿—but who are they that will do it? Who is it that will sacrifice his life for his country? Will the senator who supports a war? Will the writer who declaims upon patriotism? Will the minister of religion who recommends the sacrifice? Take away glory﻿—take away *war*, and there is not a man of them who will do it. Will you sacrifice your life at *home*? If the loss of your life in London or at York, would procure just so much benefit to your country, as the loss of one soldier in the field, would you be willing to lay your head upon the block? Are you willing to die without notice and without remembrance; and for the sake of this little undiscoverable contribution to your country’s good. You would, perhaps, die to *save* your country; but this is not the question. A soldier’s death does not save his country. The question is, whether, without any of the circumstances of war, without any of its glory or its pomp, you are willing to resign yourself to the executioner. If you are not, you are not willing to die for your country. And there is not an individual amongst the thousands who declaim upon patriotism, who is willing to do it. He will lay down his life, indeed﻿—but it must be in war; he is willing to die﻿—but it is not for patriotism, but for glory.

The argument we think is clear﻿—that patriotism is **not** the motive; and that in no rational use of language can it be said that the soldier “dies for his country.” Men will not sacrifice their lives at all, unless it be in war, and they do not sacrifice them in war from motives of patriotism.11

What, then, is the foundation of military fame? Is it bravery? Bravery has little connection with reason, and less with religion. Intellect may despise, and Christianity condemns it. Is it patriotism? Do we refer to the soldier’s motives and purposes? If we do, he is not, necessarily or often, a patriot. It was a common expression amongst sailors, and, perhaps, may be so still﻿—“I hate the French, because they are slaves, and wear wooden shoes.” This was the sum of their reasonings and their patriotism; and I do not think the mass of those who fight on land, possess a greater.

===Chinese===

「为我们祖国的自由、法律与宗教牺牲生命」，这无疑是冠冕堂皇的说辞：——但究竟何人会身体力行？究竟何人会为了他的国家牺牲自己的生命？是支持战争的参议员吗？是慷慨陈词宣讲爱国主义的作家吗？还是倡导牺牲的宗教牧师？拿走荣耀——拿走战争，他们当中没有一个人愿意这样做。你愿意在自己的故土牺牲生命吗？如果在伦敦或约克失去你的生命，所能为你的国家带来的利益，恰好等同于战场上失去一名士兵所带来的利益，你愿意将自己的头颅放在断头台上吗？你愿意默默无闻、不留痕迹地死去，仅仅为了对你的国家做出这点难以察觉的贡献吗？答案是否定的，全人类都会回答否。那么，当人们在战场上自愿冒生命危险时，这是怎么一回事呢？因为他们是在受到荣耀的强烈诱惑下这样做的。如果荣耀是其动机，爱国主义就不是。然而，不仅荣耀不是爱国主义，甚至它通常与爱国主义格格不入。我们如果探求普通士兵的行为动机，难道我们会审视他的动机与目的吗？倘若审视，他绝非必然或经常是一位爱国者。水手之间曾有一句口头禅，或许如今依然如此——「我恨法国人，因为他们是奴隶，还穿着木屐。」这就是他们全部的推论与爱国情怀；而我认为陆上作战的大众所拥有的爱国情怀，也不会比这多到哪去。11

===Original===

Crimes should be traced to their causes; and guilt should be fixed upon those who occasion, although they may not perpetrate them. And to whom are the frequency and the crimes of war to be principally attributed? To the directors of public opinion, to the declaimers upon glory﻿—to men who sit quietly at home in their studies and at their desks; to the historian, and the biographer, and the poet, and the moral philosopher; to the pamphleteer; to the editor of the newspaper; to the teacher of religion. One example of declamation from the pulpit I would offer to the reader:﻿—“Go then, ye defenders of your country; advance, with alacrity, into the field, where God himself musters the hosts to war. Religion is too much interested in your success, not to lend you her aid. She will shed over this enterprise her selectest influence. I cannot but imagine, the virtuous heroes, legislators and patriots, of every age and country, are bending from their elevated seats to witness this contest, as if they were incapable, till it be brought to a favorable issue, of enjoying their eternal repose. Enjoy that repose, illustrious immortals! Your mantle fell when you ascended, and thousands, inflamed with spirit, and impatient to tread in your steps, are ready to swear by Him that sitteth upon the throne, and liveth forever and ever, they will protect freedom in her last asylum, and never desert that cause which you sustained by your labors, and cemented with your blood. And thou, sole Ruler among the children of men, to whom the shields of the earth belong. Gird on thy sword, thou most Mighty. Go forth with our hosts in the day of battle! Impart, in addition to their hereditary valor, that confidence of success which springs from thy presence! Pour into their hearts the spirit of departed heroes! Inspire them with thine own; and while led by thine hand, and fighting under thy banners, open thou their eyes to behold in every valley, and in every plain, what the prophet beheld by the same illumination﻿—chariots of fire and horses of fire. Then shall the strong man be as tow, and the maker of it as a spark; and they shall both burn together, and none shall quench them!”12 Of such irreverence of language, employed to convey such violence of sentiment, the world, I hope, has had few examples. Oh!

===Chinese===

罪行理应追溯其源头；罪责应当归于那些促成罪行的人，尽管他们未必亲自动手。那么，战争的频发与罪行主要应归咎于何人呢？归咎于公众舆论的引导者，归咎于荣耀的颂扬者——归咎于那些安坐在家中书斋和案牍前的人们；归咎于历史学家、传记作家、诗人和道德哲学家；归咎于政论小册子作者；归咎于报纸主编；归咎于宗教导师。我愿向读者提供一个讲坛宣讲的例证：——「去吧，祖国的卫士们；敏捷地迈向战场吧，上帝亲自在那里整饬战阵。宗教与你们的胜利休戚相关，绝不会吝于施以援手。她将把最精选的恩典倾注于这项事业。我禁不住想象，历代各邦品德高尚的英雄、立法者与爱国志士，正从其崇高的天座俯身凝视这场竞赛，仿佛在这场争战得出圆满结果之前，他们便无法享受永恒的安息。享受那安息吧，辉煌不朽的英灵们！你们升天时外袍已经落下，成千上万意气风发、急欲追随你们足迹的人，随时准备凭着那坐在宝座上、活到永永远远的主起誓，他们将在自由最后的避难所捍卫自由，绝不背弃你们以辛劳维持、以鲜血铸就的事业。而你，人类儿女唯一的统治者，大地的盾牌皆属于你。全能的主啊，束上你的利剑吧。在交战之日与我们的军队同行吧！除了他们世代相传的勇气之外，更赐予他们因你同在而生的必胜信念！将昔日英雄的气魄倾注于他们的心间！以你自己的圣灵启迪他们；当他们在你的圣手引领下、在你的旗帜下奋战时，开启他们的双眼，让他们在每个山谷、每个平原，看见先知在同样灵光启示下所目睹的景象——火马与火车。那时，强人必如麻绒，他的作为必如火星；二者一同焚烧，无人扑灭！」12 运用如此不敬的语言来传达如此狂暴的情感，我希望世间罕有其例。噢！

===Original===

how unlike another exhortation﻿—“Put on mercies, kindness, humbleness of mind, meekness, long-suffering, forbearing one another, and forgiving one another, if any man have a quarrel against any.”13

“As long as mankind,” says Gibbon, “shall continue to bestow more liberal applause on their destroyers than on their benefactors, the thirst of military glory will ever be the vice of the most exalted characters.”14 “ ’Tis strange to imagine,” says the Earl of Shaftesbury, “that war, which of all things appears the most savage, should be the passion of the most heroic spirits.”﻿—But he gives us the reason.﻿—“By a small *misguidance of the affection*, a lover of mankind becomes a ravager; a hero and deliverer becomes an oppressor and destroyer.”15 This is the “vice,” and this is the “misguidance,” which we say, that a large proportion of the writers of every civilized country are continually occasioning and promoting; and thus, without, perhaps any purpose of mischief, they contribute more to the destruction of mankind than rapine or ambition. A writer thinks, perhaps, that it is not much harm to applaud bravery. The divergency from virtue may, indeed, be small in its beginning, but the effect of his applauses proceeds in the line of obliquity, until it conducts at last, to every excess of outrage, to every variety of crime, to every mode of human destruction.

There is one species of declamation on the glories of those who die in battle, to which I would beg the notice of the reader. We are told that when the last breath of exultation and defiance is departed, *the intrepid spirit rises triumphantly from the field of glory to its kindred heavens*. What the hero has been on earth, it matters not: if he dies by a musket ball, he enters heaven in his own right. All men like to suppose that they shall attain felicity at last; and to find that they can attain it without goodness and in spite of vice, is doubtless peculiarly solacing. The history of the hero’s achievements wants, indeed, a completeness without it; and this gratuitous transfer of his soul to heaven, forms an agreeable conclusion to his story.

===Chinese===

与另一句劝诫何其截然不同——「要存怜悯、恩慈、谦虚、温柔、忍耐的心。倘若这人与那人有嫌隙，总要彼此包容，彼此饶恕。」13

正如吉本（Gibbon）所言：「只要人类继续对毁灭他们的人给予比对其恩人更慷慨的赞誉，对军事荣耀的渴求就永远是最高尚人物的恶疾。」14 沙夫茨伯里伯爵（Earl of Shaftesbury）曾说：「令人难以想象的是，战争这看似最为野蛮之事，竟然会成为最英武灵魂的狂热激情。」——但他给出了原因所在。——「只需对情感略加误导，一个热爱人类的人就会变成蹂躏者；一个英雄和解救者就会变成压迫者和毁灭者。」15 这便是那种『恶疾』，这便是那种『误导』，我们说，每个文明国度相当大比例的作家都在不断促成和助长这一恶疾；因而，或许他们毫无害人之心，却比掠夺或野心对人类的毁灭做出了更大的贡献。一位作家或许认为，赞美勇猛并无大碍。美德的偏离在最初或许确实微乎其微，但他赞美的效果却沿着倾斜的轨道不断延展，直至最终导向穷凶极恶的暴行、林林总总的罪恶，以及形形色色的对人类的毁灭。

关于战死沙场者的荣耀，有一类宣叙言论我恳请读者予以注意。有人告诉我们，当欢呼与挑战的最后一口气吐出时，勇毅的灵魂便从荣耀的战场胜利升入与之相通的天国。英雄在世上为人如何无关紧要：只要他死于滑膛枪子弹，他便凭自身权利升入天堂。所有人都乐于设想自己终将获得福乐；而发现无需良善且即便劣迹斑斑亦能获得福乐，无疑尤为令人宽慰。若无这一笔，英雄事迹的记录确实缺乏完备性；而这种将其灵魂平白无故移送天国的做法，为他的故事构成了一个令人愉悦的结语。

===Original===

I would be far from “dealing damnation round the land,” and undoubtingly believe that of those who fall in battle, many have found an everlasting resting-place. But an indiscriminate consignment of the brave to felicity, is certainly unwarranted; and if wickedness consists in the promotion of wickedness, it is wicked too.

If we say in positive and glowing language, of men indiscriminately, and therefore of the bad, that they *rise on the wings of ecstasy to heaven*, we do all that language can do in the encouragement of profligacy. The terrors of religion *may* still be dreaded; but we have, at least to the utmost of our power, diminished their influence. The mind willingly accepts the assurance, or acquiesces in the falsehood which it wishes to be true; and in spite of all their better knowledge, it may be feared that some continue in profligacy, in the doubting hope that what poets and historians tell them may not be a fiction.

Perhaps the most operative encouragement which these declamations give to the soldier’s vices, is contained in this circumstance﻿—that they manifest that public opinion does not hold them in abhorrence. Public opinion is one of the most efficacious regulators of the passions of mankind; and upon the soldier this rein is peculiarly influential. His profession and his personal conduct derive almost all their value and their reputation from the opinion of the world, and from that alone. If, therefore, the public voice does not censure his vices﻿—if, in spite of his vices, it awards him everlasting happiness, what restraint remains upon his passions, or what is the wonder if they be not restrained?

===Chinese===

我绝非要在「四方宣判永罚」，并且毫无疑问地相信，在阵亡将士中，许多人已寻得了永恒的栖所。然而，不加区分地断定勇者尽享福乐，无疑是毫无根据的；如果邪恶在于助长邪恶，那么这种做法本身也是邪恶的。

如果我们用明确而热烈的语言，不加区分地对所有人（因而包括恶人）宣称他们乘着狂喜的翅膀飞向天国，我们就是在以言辞所能达到的极限鼓励放荡罪行。宗教的恐怖或许仍令人畏惧；但我们至少已竭尽所能削弱了其影响力。人心乐于接受保证，或默认其渴望成真的谎言；尽管心知肚明，令人担忧的是，某些人仍在放荡中沉沦，心怀疑虑地指望诗人和历史学家所言或许并非虚构。

这些宣叙言论对军人士兵之恶习最有效的鼓动，或许就在于这一事实——它们表明公众舆论并未对这些恶习深恶痛绝。公众舆论是调节人类激情的最有效力量之一；而对士兵而言，这一缰绳具有格外强大的影响力。他的职业和他个人的品行，其全部价值与声誉几乎完全源于世人的评价，且唯独源于此。因此，如果公众的声音不谴责他的恶习——如果在其劣迹斑斑的情况下仍赐予他永恒的幸福，对其激情的约束还剩下什么？如果他的激情未受约束，又有什么好奇怪的呢？

===Original===

The peculiar application of the subject to our purpose is, however, that these and similar representations are motives to the profession of arms. The military life is made a privileged profession, in which a man may indulge vices with impunity. His occupation is an apology for his crimes, and shields them from punishment. And what greater motive to the military life can be given? Or what can be more atrocious than the crime of those who give it? I know not, indeed, whether the guilt predominates, or the folly. Pitiable imbecility surely it is, that can persuade itself to sacrifice all the beauties of virtue, and all the realities and terrors of religion, to the love of the flowing imagery of *spirits ascending to heaven*. Whether writers shall do this is a question, not of choice, but of duty: if we would not be the abettors of crime, and the sharers of its guilt, it is imperative that we refrain.

===Chinese===

然而，这一论题对我们宗旨的特殊适用之处在于：这些言论及类似描绘构成了投身军旅职业的动机。军旅生活被塑造成一种享有特权的职业，在其中人们可以纵情作恶而不受惩罚。他的职业为其罪行开脱，并庇护这些罪行免受制裁。还能给予军旅生活什么更大的动机呢？或者还有什么能比提供这种动机者的罪行更为可恶？事实上，我不知道究竟是罪恶占据了上风，还是愚蠢占据了上风。自欺欺人地为了贪恋灵魂飞升天国的华丽意象，竟将一切美德的精华以及宗教的全部现实与戒惧牺牲殆尽，委实是可悲的愚钝。作家们是否应当这样做，并非一个选择问题，而是一个责任问题：如果我们不想成为罪行的教唆者和罪责的分担者，就必须克制自己。

===Original===

The reader will, perhaps, have observed that some of those writers who are liberal contributors to the military passion, occasionally, in moments when truth and nature seem to have burst the influence of habit, emphatically condemn the system which they have so often contributed to support. There are not many books of which the tendency is more warlike, or which are more likely to stimulate the passion for martial glory, than the *Life of Nelson*, by Southey; a work, in the composition of which, it probably never suggested itself to the author to inquire whether he were not contributing to the destruction of mankind. A contributor, however, as he has been, we find in another of his works, this extraordinary and memorable passage: “There is but one community of Christians in the world, and that, unhappily, of all communities one of the smallest, enlightened enough to understand the *prohibition of war by our Divine Master*, in its *plain, literal, and undeniable sense*; and conscientious enough to obey it, subduing the very instinct of nature to obedience.”16 Of these voluntary or involuntary testimonies of the mind against the principles which it habitually possesses, and habitually inculcates, many examples might be given;17 and they are valuable testimonies, because they appear to be elicited by the influence of simple nature and unclouded truth. This, I think, is their obvious character. They will commonly be found to have been written when the mind has become sobered by reason, or tranquillized by religion; when the feelings are not excited by external stimulants, and when conquest and honor and glory are reduced to that station of importance to which truth assigns them.

But whether such testimonies have much tendency to give conviction to a reader, I know not. Surrounded as they are with a general contrariety of sentiment, it is possible that those who read them may pass them by as the speculations of impracticable morality. I cannot, however, avoid recommending the reader, whenever he meets with passages like these, seriously to examine into their meaning and their force: to inquire whether they be not accordant with the purity of truth, and whether they do not possess the greater authority, because they have forced themselves from the mind when least likely to be deceived, and in opposition to all its habits and all its associations.

===Chinese===

读者或许已经注意到，某些极力助长军事狂热的作家，偶尔在真理与天性似乎挣脱了习惯影响的时刻，也会断然谴责他们经常襄助支持的这一体制。在激发尚武狂热方面，倾向更为好战的书并不多见于骚塞（Southey）所著的《纳尔逊传》（Life of Nelson）；在这部作品的创作中，作者大概从未想过探究自己是否在助长对人类的毁灭。然而，尽管他曾是助长者，我们在他的另一部作品中却发现了这段非同寻常且令人难忘的论述：「世上唯有一个基督徒社群，且不幸的是，在所有社群中它是最小的社群之一，具有足够的启蒙去理解我们神圣导师对战争的禁令（prohibition of war by our Divine Master），就其纯朴、字面且不可否认的意义（plain, literal, and undeniable sense）而言；并具有足够的良知去遵行它，使天性的本能顺服于这种顺从。」16 至于人心自发或非自发地针对其习以为常拥有并习惯性灌输之原则所作的这些见证，可以举出许多例证；17 它们是宝贵的见证，因为它们似乎是在朴素的天性与清澈的真理影响下被激发的。我认为，这正是其显而易见的特质。通常会发现，这些文字是在头脑被理性恢复清醒、或被宗教抚平心境之时写就的；当时其情感未受外界刺激的挑动，征服、荣誉与光荣被降到了真理赋予它们的应有地位。

然而，此类见证是否能在很大程度上使读者信服，我不得而知。由于置身于普遍对立的情感包围之中，阅读这些文字的人很有可能将其当作无法付诸实践之道德的空谈而弃之一旁。然而，每当读者遇到类似段落时，我禁不住建议读者严肃审视其含义与力量：探究它们是否与真理的纯洁相一致，是否具有更大的权威性——因为它们是在最不可能受到欺骗的时刻、在与心灵的一切习惯和一切联想截然对立的情形下从心底迸发出来的。

===Original===

Such, then, are amongst the principal of the causes of war. Some consist in want of thought, and some in delusion; some are mercenary, and some simply criminal. Whether any or all of them form a motive to the desolation of empires and to human destruction, such as a good or a reasoning man, who abstracts himself from habitual feelings, can contemplate with approbation, is a question which everyone should ask and determine for himself. A conflict of nations is a serious thing: no motive arising from our passions should occasion it, or have any influence in occasioning it: supposing the question of *lawfulness* to be superseded, war should be imposed only by stern, inevitable, unyielding necessity. That such a necessity is contained in these motives, I think cannot be shown. We may, therefore, reasonably question the defensibility of the custom, which is continued by such causes, and supported with such motives. If a tree is known by its fruits, we may also judge the fruit by the tree: “Men do not gather grapes of thorns.” If the motives to war and its causes are impure, war itself cannot be virtuous; and I would, therefore, solemnly invite the reader to give, to the succeeding Inquiry, his sober and Christian attention.

===Chinese===

以上便是战争的主要起因。有些起因源于缺乏深思，有些起因源于受蒙骗；有些起因唯利是图，有些则纯属犯罪。它们中的任何一种或全部，是否构成了使得一个超脱于习惯性情感的善人或理性之人能够赞许地看待帝国荒芜与人类毁灭的动机，这是一个每个人都应当自我追问并作出决断的问题。国与国之间的冲突非同小可：任何源自我们激情的动机都不应引发冲突，或对引发冲突产生任何影响；假定合法性（lawfulness）的问题已被搁置，战争也唯有在严酷、不可避免、毫不退让的必要性迫使下才应被施加。我认为无法证明这些动机中包含着这样的必要性。因此，我们可以合理地质疑这一由如此起因所维系、由如此动机所支撑之习俗的正当防卫性。既然凭果子认树，我们亦可凭树断果：「荆棘上岂能摘葡萄呢？」倘若战争的动机及其起因是不纯的，战争本身便不可能是道德高尚的；因此，我郑重邀请读者，以清醒和基督徒的专注，投入随后的探究之中。

## II / 第二章
===Original===

An Inquiry, etc.

When I endeavor to divest myself of the influence of habit, and to contemplate a battle with those emotions which it would excite in the mind of a being who had never before heard of human slaughter, I find that I am impressed only with horror and astonishment; and perhaps, of the two emotions, astonishment is the greater.

That several thousand persons should meet together, and then deliberately begin to kill one another, appears to the understanding a proceeding so preposterous, so monstrous, that I think a being such as I have supposed, would inevitably conclude that they were mad. Nor, if it were attempted to explain to him some motives to such conduct, do I believe that he would be able to comprehend how any possible circumstances could make it reasonable. The ferocity and prodigious folly of the act would outbalance the weight of every conceivable motive, and he would turn, unsatisfied, away,

“Astonished at the madness of mankind.”

There is an advantage in making suppositions such as these; because, when the mind has been familiarized to a practice, however monstrous or inhuman, it loses some of its sagacity of moral perception﻿—profligacy becomes honor, and inhumanity becomes spirit. But if the subject is by some circumstance presented to the mind unconnected with any of its previous associations, we see it with a new judgment and new feelings; and wonder, perhaps, that we have not felt so or thought so before. And such occasions it is the part of a wise man to seek; since if they never happen to us, it will often be difficult for us accurately to estimate the qualities of human actions, or to determine whether we approve them from a decision of our judgment, or whether we yield to them only the acquiescence of habit.

It is worthy at least of notice and remembrance, that the only being in the creation of Providence which engages in the wholesale destruction of his own species, is man; that being who alone possesses reason to direct his conduct, who alone is required to love his fellows, and who alone hopes in futurity for repose and peace. All this seems wonderful, and may reasonably humiliate us. The powers which elevate us above the rest of the creation, we have employed in attaining to preeminence of outrage and malignity.

===Chinese===

论战争与基督教原则的契合性等

当我竭力摆脱习俗的影响，并以一个从未听闻过人类屠杀之生灵的心境去凝视一场战役时，我发现自己心中唯有惊骇与错愕；而在两种情感中，错愕或许尤甚于惊骇。

数千人聚集在一起，然后从容不迫地开始自相残杀，在理智看来这是一种如此荒谬绝伦、如此骇人听闻的行径，以至于我认为，我所假想的那种生灵必将断定他们全都疯了。即便有人试图向他解释此类行径的某些动机，我也绝不相信他能理解在任何可能的情形下这种行径何以变得合情合理。此举的凶残与巨大的愚昧将压倒任何可以想象的动机的分量，而他必将大失所望，悻悻离去，

「惊诧于人类的疯狂。」

做出此类设想是大有裨益的；因为当心灵对一种习俗司空见惯时，不论该习俗何等怪异或不仁，心灵都会丧失其道德感知的敏锐——放荡成了荣耀，不仁成了气魄。然而，如果因某种机缘，这一事物以脱离了其以往一切关联的方式呈现在心灵面前，我们便会以全新的判断和感受去审视它；并且或许会惊异，自己以往何以竟未曾如此感受或思考。而此类契机正是智者应当寻觅的；因为倘若我们从未经历这些契机，我们往往便难以准确评估人类行为的品格，也难以断定我们认可这些行为究竟是出于明智的裁决，还是仅仅屈从于习俗的默许。

在天命（Providence）所造的万物之中，唯一从事大规模灭绝同类的受造物唯有人类，这一点至少值得引起人们的注意与铭记；唯有人类拥有理性以指引其行为，唯有人类被要求去爱同胞，唯有人类企盼在未来获得安息与和平。这一切显得令人惊异，也理应使我们蒙羞。那些使我们超越受造万物的禀赋，反倒被我们用来在残暴与恶毒上登峰造极。

===Original===

It may properly be a subject of wonder, that the arguments which are brought to justify a custom such as war receive so little investigation. It must be a studious ingenuity of mischief, which could devise a practice more calamitous or horrible; and yet it is a practice of which it rarely occurs to us to inquire into the necessity, or to ask whether it cannot be or ought not to be avoided. In one truth, however, all will acquiesce, that the arguments in favor of such a practice should be unanswerably strong.

Let it not be said that the experience and the practice of other ages have superseded the necessity of inquiry in our own; that there can be no reason to question the lawfulness of that which has been sanctioned by forty centuries; or that he who presumes to question it is amusing himself with schemes of visionary philanthropy. “There is not, it may be,” says Lord Clarendon, “a greater obstruction to the investigation of truth, or the improvement of knowledge, than the too frequent appeal, and the too supine resignation of our understanding to antiquity.”18 Whosoever proposes an alteration of existing institutions, will meet, from some men, with a sort of instinctive opposition, which appears to be influenced by no process of reasoning, by no considerations of propriety or principles of rectitude, which defends the existing system because it exists, and which would have equally defended its opposite if that had been the oldest. “Nor is it out of modesty that we have this resignation, or that we do, in truth, think those who have gone before us to be wiser than ourselves; we are as proud and as peevish as any of our progenitors; but it is out of laziness; we will rather take their words than take the pains to examine the reason they governed themselves by.”19 To those who urge objections from the authority of ages, it is, indeed, a sufficient answer to say that they apply to every long-continued custom. Slave-dealers urged them against the friends of the abolition; Papists urged them against Wickliffe and Luther; and the Athenians probably thought it a good objection to an apostle, that “he seemed to be a setter forth of *strange* gods.”

===Chinese===

人们用来为战争这一习俗辩护的论点竟受到如此少的研究，这实在应当成为一件令人惊异之事。唯有用心良苦的作恶巧思，方能构想出一种比战争更为灾难深重、更为骇人听闻的习俗；然而，我们却鲜少想到去探究这一习俗的必要性，或去追问它是否并非不可避免、是否本应加以避免。不过，众人必当认同这样一个真理：支持这一习俗的论据理应无可辩驳地确凿有力。

切莫说什么前代的经验与践行已取代了我们当世探究的必要；切莫说什么受到四十个世纪认可的事物，就断无理由去质疑其合法性；亦切莫说什么胆敢质疑之人，不过是在沉迷于空想博爱主义的虚妄构想。「或许，」克拉伦登勋爵（Lord Clarendon）说道，「对于探求真理或增进知识而言，最大的阻碍莫过于频繁诉诸古代，并使我们的悟性过于怠惰地屈从于古代。」18 凡提出变革现行体制者，都会遭到某些人本能般的反对；这种反对似乎不受任何推理过程的影响，亦不受任何得体考量或正直原则的制约，它之所以捍卫现行制度仅仅因为其存在，且倘若现行制度的对立面更加古老，它也会同样去加以捍卫。「我们有此屈从，亦非出于谦逊，抑或我们在心底里真切认为前人比我们更为明智；我们与我们的任何先祖一样骄傲而乖戾；这实乃出于怠惰；我们宁可听信他们的话语，也不愿费心去审视他们赖以自律的理据。」19 对于那些借助世代的权威提出异议的人，确实只需回答：此类异议可适用于一切相沿已久的习俗。奴隶贩子曾以此反对废奴运动的支持者；教皇派天主教徒（Papists）曾以此反对威克利夫（Wickliffe）与路德（Luther）；而在雅典人看来，指责一位使徒「似乎是传扬异邦神明的」，大概也是极有力的反对理由。

===Original===

It is agreed by all sober moralists, that the foundation of our duty is the will of God, and that his will is to be ascertained by the Revelation which He has made. To Christianity, therefore, we refer in determination of this great question: we admit no other test of truth: and with him who thinks that the decisions of Christianity may be superseded by other considerations, we have no concern; we address not our argument to him, but leave him to find some other and better standard, by which to adjust his principles and regulate his conduct. These observations apply to those objectors who loosely say that “wars are necessary;” for supposing the Christian religion to prohibit war, it is preposterous, and irreverent also, to justify ourselves in supporting it, because “it is necessary.” To talk of a divine law which *must be disobeyed*, implies, indeed, such a confusion of moral principles as well as laxity of them, that neither the philosopher or the Christian are required to notice it. But, perhaps, some of those who say that wars are necessary, do not very accurately inquire what they mean. There are two sorts of necessity﻿—moral and physical; and these, it is probable, some men are accustomed to confound. That there is any physical necessity for war﻿—that people cannot, if they choose, refuse to engage in it, no one will maintain. And a *moral* necessity to perform an action, consists only in the prospect of a certain *degree* of evil by refraining from it. If, then, those who say that “wars are necessary,” mean that they are physically necessary, we deny it. If they mean that wars avert greater evils than they occasion, we ask for proof. Proof has never yet been given: and even if we thought that we possessed such proof, we should still be referred to the primary question﻿—“What is the will of God?”

===Chinese===

严谨的道德哲学家一致认同，我们责任的根基乃是上帝的旨意，而祂的旨意当由祂所赐的启示来探知。因此，在裁决这一重大问题时，我们唯有诉诸基督教：我们不承认任何其他的真理检验标准；至于那些认为基督教的裁决可被其他考量所取代的人，我们概不予以理会；我们的论辩并非针对此等人，而是任由他去寻觅某种其他且更好的标准，以调整其原则并规范其行为。这些观察适用于那些泛泛而言「战争乃是必要之举」的反对者；盖因假定基督教禁止战争，则以「战争是必要的」为由为我们支持战争辩护，不仅荒谬绝伦，而且是对神圣的亵渎。奢谈一条「必须予以违背」的神圣诫律，确实意味着对道德原则的混乱与废弛，以至于无论是哲学家还是基督徒皆无需对其予以理会。然而，在那些声称战争是必要的人当中，或许有些人并未十分确切地探究其所言究竟何意。必然性有两种——道德必然性与物理必然性；而某些人很可能习惯于将二者混为一谈。说战争存在任何物理上的必然性——即人们若有选择却无法拒绝参与战争——断无人会主张此点。而从事某一行为的「道德必然性」，仅在于人们预见到若不采取该行为便会招致某种程度的恶害。因此，倘若那些声称「战争是必要的」人，意指战争具有物理必然性，我们断然予以否认。倘若他们意指战争所避免的恶害大于其所造成的恶害，我们便要求提供证据。然而此种证据迄今从未有人给出；纵使我们自认为掌握了此类证据，我们也仍须回到那个根本问题——「上帝的旨意究竟为何？」

===Original===

It is some satisfaction to be able to give, on a question of this nature, the testimony of some great minds against the lawfulness of war, opposed as those testimonies are to the general prejudice and the general practice of the world. It has been observed by Beccaria, that “it is the fate of great truths, to glow only like a flash of lightning amidst the dark clouds in which error has enveloped the universe; and if our testimonies are few or transient, it matters not, so that their light be the light of truth.” There are, indeed, many, who in describing the horrible particulars of a siege or a battle, indulge in some declamations on the horrors of war, such as has been often repeated and often applauded, and as often forgotten. But such declamations are of little value and of little effect: he who reads the next paragraph finds, probably, that he is invited to follow the path *to glory and to victory﻿—to share the hero’s danger and partake the hero’s praise*; and he soon discovers that the moralizing parts of his author are the impulse of feelings rather than of principles, and thinks that though it may be very well to write, yet it is better to forget them.

There are however, testimonies, delivered in the calm of reflection, by acute and enlightened men, which may reasonably be allowed at least so much weight as to free the present inquiry from the charge of being wild or visionary. Christianity indeed needs no such auxiliaries; but if they induce an examination of her duties, a wise man will not wish them to be disregarded.

===Chinese===

在探讨此类性质的问题时，能够列举若干伟大心灵反对战争合法性的见证，委实令人欣慰，尽管这些见证与世间普遍的偏见及通行的践行截然相悖。贝卡利亚（Beccaria）曾指出：「伟大真理的命运，乃是在谬误笼罩宇宙的重重阴云之中，仅如一道闪电般闪烁；即使我们的见证寥若晨星或稍纵即逝，那也无关紧要，只要它们的光芒乃是真理之光。」确实有许多人在描绘围城或交战的恐怖细节时，沉溺于对战争惨状的高谈阔论，此类言辞屡见不鲜、屡获喝彩，却也同样屡被遗忘。然而，此类空谈毫无价值且收效甚微：读者翻至下一段，便很可能会发现自己受邀追随「通往荣耀与胜利之途——共担英雄的险阻并共享英雄的赞誉」；他很快便会发觉，作者那些阐发道德之语不过是一时的情感冲动，而非出于坚定原则，因而会认为写写固然无妨，但最好还是将其抛诸脑后。

然而，亦有一些见证乃是敏锐而开明之人在冷静沉思中作出的；理应赋予这些见证至少足够的分量，以使当下的探讨免于被斥为荒诞不经或不切实际之空谈。基督教固然无需此类外援；但若它们能促使人们去审视自身的基督教责任，智者便断不会希望将其视若无睹。

===Original===
