Because it “made nothing perfect.”﻿—“The law was given by Moses, but grace and *truth* came by Jesus Christ.” The manner in which the author of “truth” prefaced some of his most important precepts, is much to our present purpose. “It hath been said by them of old time, an eye for an eye,” etc. He then introduces his own precept with the contradistinguishing preface﻿—“But *I* say unto you.” This, therefore, appears to be a specific abrogation of the *authority* of the legal injunctions, and an introduction of another system; and this is all that our present purpose requires. The truth is, that the law was abolished because of its imperfections; yet we take hold of one of these imperfections in justification of our present practice. Is it because we feel that we cannot defend it by our own religion?

===Chinese===

若有人告诉我，我们今日依然承认其中诸多训诫的约束力，我便回答说，我们承认其所吩咐的本分，但绝非因为颁布这些训诫的权威。我们顺服这些诫命，并非因为它们是在律法之下颁布的，而是因为它们得到了基督教的施行与确认。「不可杀人」的诫命从未被废止；但基督徒之所以禁止谋杀，并非因为《十诫》（Decalogue）曾对其予以谴责，即便《十诫》从未存在过，他们也会予以禁止。

不仅如此，对于律法下的某些命令，基督教甚至要求我们违背。「人若有顽梗悖逆的儿子，不听从父亲的话……本城的众人就要用石头将他打死。48」「你的同胞弟兄，或是你的儿女，或是你怀中的妻……暗中引诱你，说：『我们不如去事奉别神』……你不可顾惜他，也不可遮庇他，总要杀他；你要先下手，然后众民也下手，将他治死。49」如今我们深知基督教绝不会认可对这些命令的遵从；若我们遵行它们，我们自己的法律也会将我们视作杀人犯。摩西经纶之下的训诫若因由上天颁布而具有约束力，那么它们在所有的命令与所有的禁令中便都具有约束力。然而，这些训诫中的某些条目我们习以为常地予以忽视，某些条目若去遵从便构成犯罪；那么，我们究竟凭何理由在为自己辩护时诉诸它们呢？

那么，律法为何被取代？因为律法「原来一无所成」——「律法本是藉着摩西传的，恩典和真理都是由耶稣基督来的。」这位「真理」的缔造者为其某些最重要教诫所作的开篇序言，与我们当下的论旨切切相关。「你们听见有吩咐古人的话说：『以眼还眼……』」接着，祂以对比鲜明的序言引入了自己的教诫——「只是我告诉你们。」因此，这显然是对律法训诫之权威的明确废止，并引入了另一套体系；而这已完全满足了我们当下的论述需要。事实真相是，律法因其不完全而被废除；然而我们却紧抓其中的一项不完全，用以证明我们今日做法的正当。难道这是因为我们深感无法凭借我们自己的宗教来为之辩护吗？

===Original===

We therefore dismiss the dispensation of Moses from any participation in the argument. Whatever it allowed, or whatever it prohibited, in relation to war, we do not inquire. We ask only what Christianity allows and prohibits, and by this we determine the question.﻿—It is the more necessary to point out the inapplicability of these arguments from the Old Testament, because there are some persons of desultory modes of thinking, who find that war is allowed in “the Bible,” and who forget to inquire into the present authority of the permission.

There are some persons who suppose themselves sufficiently justified in their approbation of war, by the example of men of piety of our own times. The argument, as an argument, is of little concern; but everything is important that makes us acquiescent in war. *Here are men*, say they, *who make the knowledge of their duties the great object of their study, and yet these men engage in war without any doubt of its lawfulness*. All this is true; and it is true also, that some good men have expressly inculcated the lawfulness of war; and it is true also, that the articles of the Church of England specifically assert it. But what, if it should have come to pass, that “blindness in part, hath happened unto Israel!”

===Chinese===

因此，我们将摩西经纶从本论辩的任何考量中彻底排除。关于战争，无论它曾允许何事，或禁止何事，我们皆不过问。我们仅查问基督教允许何事、禁止何事，并以此决断这一问题。——指出这些源自《旧约》之论点的不可适用性尤为必要，因为有些思维散漫之人发现战争在《圣经》中是被允许的，却忘记了探究这种许可在今日是否仍具有权威。

有些人以为，诉诸我们这个时代虔诚之士的榜样，便足以证明他们赞同战争是正当的。就其本身作为论据而言，此论点无足轻重；但凡是促使我们默认战争之事，皆不可轻视。他们说：「看哪，这些人将明晓自身本分作为其研习的重大目标，然而他们却毫不怀疑战争的合法性而投身其中。」这一切皆属实情；同样属实的是，某些良善之人曾明确宣导过战争的正当性；同样属实的是，英国国教会的信纲亦对此作了具体断言。然而，倘若事实恰如圣经所言——「以色列人有几分是硬心盲目的」呢！

===Original===

What is the argument? *That good men have engaged in war, and therefore that Christianity allows it.* They who satisfy themselves with such reasoning, should bear in mind that he who voluntarily passes over the practice of the first two centuries of Christianity, and attempts to defend himself by the practice of after and darker ages, has obviously no other motive than that he finds his religion, when vitiated and corrupt, more suitable to his purpose than it was in the days of its purity. This state of imperfection and impurity has diffused an influence upon the good, as upon the bad. I question not that some Christians of the present day who defend war, *believe* they act in accordance with their religion; just as I question not that many, who zealously bore fagots to the stake of the Christian martyrs, *believed* so too. The time has been, when those who killed good men *thought* “they did God service.” But let the succeeding declaration be applied by our present objectors﻿—“These things will they do unto you, *because they have not known the Father nor Me*.”50 Here, then, appears to be our error﻿—that we do not estimate the conduct of men by the standard of the gospel, but that we reduce the standard of the gospel to the conduct of men. That good men should fail to conform to the perfect purity of Christianity, or to *perceive* it, need not be wondered, for we have sufficient examples of it. Good men in past ages allowed many things as permitted by Christianity, which we condemn, and shall forever condemn. In the present day there are many questions of duty on which men of piety disagree. If their authority be rejected by us on other points of practice, why is it to determine the question of war? Especially why do we insist on their decisions, when they differ in their decisions themselves? If good men have allowed the lawfulness of war, good men have also denied it. We are therefore again referred to the simple evidence of religion; an evidence which it will always be found wise to admit, and dangerous to question.

===Chinese===

这一论点究竟为何？即「良善之人曾投身战争，因此基督教允许战争」。凡满足于此类推论的人应当铭记：若有人蓄意略过基督教最初 2 个世纪的实践，企图以其后更黑暗时代的实践来为自己辩护，其动机显然别无其他——不过是他发现当自己的宗教遭到玷污和腐蚀时，比其处于纯洁之日更符合其意图。这种不完全与不纯洁的状态，既影响了恶人，亦将影响蔓延至善人身上。我毫不怀疑当今某些为战争辩护的基督徒，确信自己的行为符合其宗教信仰；正如我毫不怀疑，当年许多极其热心将柴薪搬到基督教殉道者火刑柱旁的人，同样也是如此确信的。曾几何时，那些残害良善之人的人，以为自己是「在事奉上帝」。然而，且让今日的反辩者领受接下来的宣告吧——「他们做这些事，是因为他们未曾认识父，也未曾认识我。」50 因此，我们的症结恰恰在于——我们未曾以福音的标准来衡量人的行径，反而将福音的标准贬抑至人的行径。良善之人竟未能顺应基督教的完全纯洁、抑或未能领会这种纯洁，对此无需感到惊异，因为我们已有充塞于史的例证。在以往世代，善人曾容许许多自以为基督教所准许的事物，而那些事物如今被我们所谴责，且将永远被谴责。在当今时代，在诸多关乎本分的议题上，虔诚之士亦各执一词。倘若我们在其他践履要点上拒绝以他们的权威为依归，为何偏偏要由他们来决断战争的问题？尤其是，当他们彼此之间的决断尚且大相径庭之时，我们为何还要执着于他们的决断？若有良善之人曾承认战争的合法性，亦有良善之人曾断然否认之。因此，我们再次被引向宗教本身的纯粹明证；凡接纳这明证之人必显为明智，而对其妄加质疑之人必陷于凶险。

===Original===

There is, however, one argument brought against us, which if it be just, precludes at once all question upon the subject:﻿—*That a distinction is to be made between rules which apply to us as individuals, and rules which apply to us as subjects of the state; and that the pacific injunctions of Christ from the mount, and all the other kindred commands and prohibitions of the Christian Scriptures, have no reference to our conduct as members of the political body.* This is the argument to which the greatest importance is attached by the advocates of war, and by which thinking men are chiefly induced to acquiesce in its lawfulness. In reality, some of those who think most acutely upon the subject, acknowledge that the peaceable, forbearing, forgiving dispositions of Christianity, are absolutely obligatory upon individuals in their full extent; and this acknowledgment I would entreat the reader to bear in his recollection.

Now it is obvious that the proof of the rectitude of this distinction, must be expected of those who make it. General rules are laid down by Christianity, of which, in some cases, the advocate of war denies the applicability. *He*, therefore, is to produce the reason and the authority for exception. Now we would remind him that general rules are binding, unless their inapplicability can be clearly shown. We would remind him that the general rules in question, are laid down by the commissioned ministers of Jesus Christ, and by Jesus Christ himself; and we would recommend him, therefore, to hesitate before he institutes exceptions to those rules, upon any authority *inferior* to the authority which made them.

The foundation for the distinction between the duties of Individuals, and those of Communities, must, we suppose, be sought in one of these two positions:

-

That as no law exists, of general authority amongst nations, by which one state is protected from the violence of another, it is necessary that each independent community should protect itself; and that the security of a nation cannot sometimes be maintained otherwise than by war.

-

That as the general utility and expediency of actions is the foundation of their moral qualities, and as it is sometimes most conducive to general utility and expediency that there should be war, war is, therefore, sometimes lawful.

===Chinese===

然而，有一种反对我们的论调，倘若它是正当的，便会彻底排除对该主题的一切质问：——即必须对适用于我们作为个人的规则，与适用于我们作为国家臣民的规则加以区分；基督在登山宝训中所提出的和平诫命，以及基督教经典中所有其他相关的命令与禁令，对我们作为政治团体成员的行径概不适用。这是战争辩护者最为重视的论点，也是有思考力的人之所以大多默认战争合法性的主要缘由。事实上，在此问题上思考最为敏锐的某些人亦承认，基督教关于和平、忍耐与宽恕的性情，对其个人而言在全部范围上具有绝对的约束力；我恳请读者将这一承认铭记于心。

显而易见，证明这种区分之正当性的责任，必然落在此类区分的提出者身上。基督教制定了普遍规则，而在某些情况下，战争辩护者却否认这些规则的适用性。因此，应当由他来提出破例的理由与权威依据。我们谨提醒他：普遍规则皆具约束力，除非能确凿证明其不适用。我们谨提醒他：所论及的普遍规则，乃是由耶稣基督所受命设立的执事以及耶稣基督亲自订立的；因此，我们奉劝他在凭借任何低于立法者本身的权威对这些规则确立例外之前，务必三思而行。

个人本分与社会群体本分之间的区分基础，依我们所见，必然须在如下两个论点中寻求其一：

-

鉴于各民族之间不存在具有普遍权威的法律以保护一国免遭另一国的暴力侵害，每个独立社会群体便必须自卫；而一国的安全有时除了诉诸战争之外便无法维系

-

鉴于行为的普遍功利与权宜乃是其道德属性的根基，且既然战争有时最有利于普遍功利与权宜，因此，战争有时是合法的

===Original===

The first of these positions will probably be thus enforced. If an individual suffers aggression, there is a Power to which he can apply that is above himself and above the aggressor; a power by which the bad passions of those around him are restrained, or by which their aggressions are punished. But amongst nations there is no acknowledged superior or common arbitrator.﻿—Even if there were, there is no way in which its decisions could be enforced, but by the sword. War, therefore, is the only means which one nation possesses of protecting itself from the aggression of another.

This, certainly, is plausible reasoning; but it happens to this argument as to many others, that it assumes *that* as established, which has not been proved, and upon the proof of which the truth of the whole argument depends. It assumes, That the reason why an individual is not permitted to use violence, is, *that the laws will use it for him*. And in this the fallacy of the position consists; for the foundation of the duty of forbearance in private life, is *not* that the laws will punish aggression, but *that Christianity requires forbearance*. Undoubtedly, if the existence of a common arbitrator were the foundation of the duty, the duty would not be binding upon nations. But that which we require to be proved is this﻿—that Christianity exonerates nations from those duties which she has imposed upon individuals. This, the present argument does not prove; and, in truth, with a singular unhappiness in its application, it assumes, in effect, that she has imposed these duties upon neither the one nor the other.

If it be said that Christianity allows to individuals some degree and kind of resistance, and that some resistance is therefore lawful to states, we do not deny it. But if it be said that the degree of lawful resistance extends to the slaughter of our fellow Christians﻿—that it extends to war﻿—we do deny it: We say that the rules of Christianity cannot, by any possible latitude of interpretation, be made to extend to it. The duty of forbearance then, is *antecedent* to all considerations respecting the condition of man; and whether he be under the protection of laws or not, the duty of forbearance is imposed.

===Chinese===

上述第一个论点或许会受到如此论证：若个人遭受侵犯，他可以向凌驾于他自身及侵略者之上的某种权柄申诉；借助这一权柄，其周围之人的恶劣激情得以受到克制，抑或其侵略行径得以受到惩罚。但在万国之间，并不存在公认的更高主宰或共同仲裁者。——即便存在，除了诉诸武力刀剑之外，亦绝无任何手段能够执行其决断。因此，战争乃是一国保护自身免遭另一国侵犯的唯一手段。

这固然是一种看似合理的论辩；然而正如许多其他论据一样，该论点也恰恰是将尚未得到证明、且整个论点的真实性所系的前提视作既成事实。它预先假定：个人之所以不被允许使用暴力，是因为法律将代其行使暴力。而该立场的谬误恰恰在此；因为在私人生活中，忍耐这一本分的根基，并不在于法律会惩处侵害，而在于基督教要求忍耐。毋庸置疑，若共同仲裁者的存在是这一本分的根基，那么该本分便对国家没有约束力。然而，我们要求证明的乃是这一点——即基督教免除了国家承担她强加于个人的那些本分。当前的论据并未证明这一点；而且实则极其拙劣的是，它在实际推演中甚至假定基督教既未将这些本分强加于个人，亦未强加于国家。

若有人说，基督教允许个人在某种程度与种类上进行抵抗，因此国家进行某种抵抗亦属合法，我们对此并不否认。但若有人说，合法抵抗的限度可以延伸至屠杀我们的基督徒同胞——延伸至战争——我们便断然否认：我们断言，无论对基督教规则作何种宽泛的解释，都绝不可能将其延伸至这一地步。因此，忍耐的本分先于关于人类境况的一切考量；无论一个人是否处于法律的庇护之下，忍耐的本分都被不可推卸地赋予了他。

===Original===

The only truth which appears to be elicited by the present argument is, that the difficulty of obeying the forbearing rules of Christianity, is *greater* in the case of nations than in the case of individuals: *The obligation to obey them is the same in both.* Nor let anyone urge the difficulty of obedience in opposition to the duty; for he who does this, has yet to learn one of the most awful rules of his religion﻿—a rule that was enforced by the precepts, and more especially by the final example, of Christ, of apostles, and of martyrs, the rule which requires that we should be “obedient even unto death.”

Let it not, however, be supposed that we believe the difficulty of forbearance would be as great in practice as it is great in theory. We hope hereafter to show that it promotes our interests as certainly as it fulfils our duties.

The rectitude of the distinction between rules which apply to individuals and rules which apply to states, is thus maintained by Dr. Paley on the principle of **expediency**.

===Chinese===

当前这一论证唯一能够引申出的真理，乃是遵从基督教忍耐克制法则的难度，在国家层面比在个人层面更大：遵守这些法则的义务在二者中是完全相同的。任何人皆不可因顺服之艰难而对抗这项职责；因为凡如此行事之人，尚未领悟其信仰中最为庄严可畏的一条诫律——一条由基督、使徒以及殉道者的教诲、尤其是他们最终的榜样所坚立的法则，即要求我们「顺服以至于死」的法则。

然而，切莫以为我们相信克制的难度在实践中会如在理论上那样巨大。我们希望在后文中显明，它促进我们的利益，正如它履行我们的职责一样确凿无疑。

适用于个人的准则与适用于国家的准则之间的界分之正当性，佩利博士（Dr. Paley）便是如此基于权宜（expediency）原则予以维护的

===Original===

“The *only* distinction,” says he, “that exists between the case of independent states and independent individuals, is founded in this circumstance; that the particular consequence sometimes appears to exceed the value of the general rule;” or, in less technical words, that a greater disadvantage may arise from obeying the commands of Christianity, than from transgressing them. *Expediency*, it is said, is the test of moral rectitude, and the standard of our duty. If we believe that it will be most expedient to disregard the general obligations of Christianity, that belief is the justifying motive of disregarding them. Dr. Paley proceeds to say, “In the transactions of private persons, no advantage that results from the breach of a general law of justice, can compensate to the public for the violation of the law; *in the concerns of empire* this *may sometimes be doubted*.” He says there may be cases in which “the magnitude of the particular evil induces us to *call in question* the obligation of the general rule.” “Situations *may be feigned*, and consequently *may possibly arise*, in which the general tendency is outweighed by the enormity of the particular mischief.” Of the doubts which must arise as to the occasions when the “obligation” of Christian law ceases, he however says that “moral philosophy furnishes no precise solution;” and he candidly acknowledges “the danger of leaving it to the sufferer to decide upon the comparison of particular and general consequences, and the still greater danger of such decisions being drawn into future precedents. If treaties, for instance, be no longer binding than while they are convenient, or until the inconveniency ascend to a certain point (which point must be fixed by the judgment, or rather by the feelings of the complaining party), one, and almost the only method of averting or closing the calamities of war, of preventing or putting a stop to the destruction of mankind, is lost to the world forever.” And in retrospect of the indeterminateness of these rules of conduct, he says finally, “these, however, are the principles upon which the calculation is to be formed.”51

===Chinese===

「独立国家与独立个人之间所存在的唯一界分，」他说，「乃是建立在这样一种情形之上：个别后果有时似乎超过了一般准则的价值；」或者用不那么专业的话来说，即遵从基督教的诫命可能比违背它们招致更大的不利。据说，权宜乃是道德正当性的试金石，亦是我们义务的准绳。倘若我们认为漠视基督教的一般义务最为权宜合算，这种信念本身便成了漠视这些义务的正当动机。佩利博士进而说道：「在私人交往中，违背公义的一般法则所带来的任何利益，皆不足以补偿公众因该法则遭到破坏而承受的损失；但在帝国的事务中，这一点有时或存疑问。」他指出，在某些情况下，「个别之恶的严重程度促使我们去质疑一般法则的约束力。」「人们可以设想出、因而也可能确实会出现某些情境，在其中，一般的倾向被特定祸患的巨大程度所压倒。」至于当基督教法则的『约束力』在何种场合终止这一必然产生的疑问，他却表示「道德哲学给不出确切的解答」；并且他坦率承认，「任由受害方去权衡比较个别后果与一般后果是危险的，而此类决定被援引为未来判例的危险更是有过之而无不及。例如，条约若唯有在便利时才具有约束力，或者一旦不便达到某种程度（而这一界限必然由申诉方的判断、抑或由其情绪来定夺）便失去效力，那么避免或终止战争灾难、防止或制止毁灭人类的几乎唯一的途径，就将永远从世间丧失。」在回顾了这些行为准则的不确定性之后，他最终说道：「然而，这些便是进行此种权衡计算所依据的原则。」51

===Original===

It is obvious that this reasoning proceeds upon the principle that *it is lawful to do evil that good may come*. If good will come by violating a treaty, we may violate it.52 If good will come by slaughtering other men, we may slaughter them. I know that the advocate of expediency will tell us that that is *not* evil of which good, in the aggregate, comes; and that the good or evil of actions *consists* in the good or evil of their general consequences.﻿—I appeal to the understanding and the conscience of the reader﻿—Is this distinction honest to the meaning of the apostle? Did he intend to tell his readers that they might violate their solemn promises, that they might destroy their fellow Christians, *in order that good might come*? If he did mean this, surely there was little truth in the declaration of the same apostle, *that he used great plainness of speech*.

We are told that “whatever is *expedient* is right.” We shall not quarrel with the dogma, but how is expediency to be determined? By the calculations and guessings of men, or by the knowledge and foresight of God? Expediency may be the test of our duties, but what is the test of expediency?﻿—Obviously, I think, it is this: *the decisions which God has made known respecting what is best for man*. Calculations of expediency, of “particular and general consequences,” are not entrusted to us, for this most satisfactory reason﻿—that we cannot make them. The calculation, to be anything better than vague guessing, requires prescience, and where is prescience to be sought? Now it is conceded by our opponents, that the only possessor of prescience has declared that the forbearing, non-resisting character *is* best for man. Yet we are told, that sometimes it is *not* best, that sometimes it is “inexpedient.” How do we discover this? The promulgator of the law has never intimated it. Whence, then, do we derive the right of substituting our computations for his prescience? Or, having obtained it, what is the limit to its exercise? If, because we calculate that obedience will not be beneficial, we may dispense with his laws in one instance, why may we not dispense with them in ten? Why may we not abrogate them altogether?

===Chinese===

显而易见，这种推论乃是基于「可以作恶以成善」的原则。倘若背弃条约能带来益处，我们便可以背弃它。52 倘若屠杀同胞能带来益处，我们便可以屠杀他们。我深知，权宜论的鼓吹者会告知我们，凡总体上能产生善果者便不是恶；行为的善恶完全取决于其总体后果的善恶。——我谨诉诸读者的理智与良知——这种界分是否忠实于使徒的原意？使徒的本意难道是告诉其读者，他们可以背弃庄严的诺言、可以毁灭同道的基督徒，以便成善吗？倘若这确是他的本意，那么同一位使徒所宣称的「自己行事大有坦率直言的纯朴」，便绝无多少真实性可言了。

有人对我们说：「凡权宜便利者，皆为正当。」我们无意与这一信条争辩，但权宜又当如何判定？是凭人的算计与揣测，还是凭上帝的全知与预见？权宜或许是我们职责的试金石，但权宜的试金石又是什么？——在我看来，答案显然是：上帝所昭示的关乎何者对人类至善的圣断。对权宜的计算、对「个别后果与一般后果」的权衡，并未托付给我们，其最令人信服的理由莫过于——我们根本无力进行这种计算。此种计算若想高于模糊的猜测，就必须具备预知之能，而预知之能又当向何处寻求？如今我们的对手亦承认，唯独拥有预知之能的主已昭示，坚忍克制、不加抗拒的品格对人类乃是至善的。然而有人却告诉我们，有时这并非至善，有时这是「不合权宜」的。我们是如何发现这一点的？颁布律法的主从未作过如此示意。既然如此，我们凭何获得以自身之算计僭越其预知的权利？即便获得了这种权利，其实施的界限又在何处？倘若因我们算计出顺服不会带来裨益，便可以在一件事上废免其法则，为何我们不能在十件事上废免？为何我们不能将它们彻底废除？

===Original===

The right is however claimed; and how is it to be exercised? We are told that the duty of obedience “may sometimes be *doubted*”﻿—that in some cases, we are induced to “*call in question*” the obligation of the Christian rule﻿—that “situations *may be feigned*,”﻿—that circumstances “*may possibly arise*,” in which we are at liberty to dispense with it﻿—that still it is dangerous to leave “it to the sufferer to decide,” when the obligation of the rule ceases; and that of all those doubts “philosophy furnishes no precise solution!”﻿—I know not how to contend against such principles as these. An argument might be repelled; the assertion of a fact might be disproved; but what answer can be made to “possibilities” and “doubts?” They who are at liberty to *guess* that Christian laws may sometimes be suspended, are at liberty to guess that Jupiter is a fixed star, or that the existence of America is a fiction. What answer the man of science would make to such suppositions I do not know, and I do not know what answer to make to ours. Amongst a community which had to decide on the “particular and general consequences” of some political measure, which involved the sacrifice of the principles of Christianity, there would of necessity be an endless variety of opinions. Some would think it *expedient* to supersede the law of Christianity, and some would think the evil of obeying the law less than the evil of transgressing it. Some would think that the “particular mischief” outweighed the “general rule,” and some that the “general rule” outweighed the “particular mischief.” And in this chaos of opinion, what is the line of rectitude, or how is it to be discovered? Or, is *that* rectitude, which appears to each separate individual to be right? And are there as many species of truth as there are discordances of opinion?﻿—Is *this* the simplicity of the gospel? Is *this* the path in which a wayfaring man, though a fool, shall not err?

===Chinese===

然而，这种权利却被堂而皇之地主张；但它又该如何行使？有人对我们说，顺服的义务「有时或存疑问」——在某些情况下，我们被诱导去「质疑」基督教准则的约束力——「人们可以设想出某些情境」——环境「可能确实会出现」，在其中我们得以随意免除该准则——然而在准则的约束力何时终止的问题上，任由「受害方去决定」依旧是危险的；并且对于所有这些疑虑，「哲学给不出确切的解答！」——我不知道该如何与此类原则论辩。一项论点尚可驳斥；一项事实的主张尚可证伪；但面对「种种可能」与「种种疑虑」，又能给出何种回应？那些可以任意揣测基督教法则有时可以中止的人，大可随意揣测木星（Jupiter）是一颗恒星，或者美洲（America）的存在纯属虚构。自然科学家面对此类假设会作何回应，我不得而知；而面对我们当下的这些假设，我同样不知该如何应答。在一个必须对某种涉及牺牲基督教原则的政治举措之「个别后果与一般后果」作出裁决的社群中，必然会产生无休无止的分歧意见。有人会认为废弛基督教的法则是权宜之计，有人则认为遵从法则之恶小于违背法则之恶。有人会认为「个别祸患」压倒了「一般法则」，有人则认为「一般法则」压倒了「个别祸患」。在这一片混乱的众说纷纭中，正道究竟何在，又当如何探寻？抑或，凡在每个单独个体看来正确之事，便是正道？难道意见的分歧有多少，真理的种类便也有多少？——这难道就是福音的纯朴？这难道就是那条行路之人虽愚昧亦不致走偏的坦途？

===Original===

These are the principles of expediency on which it is argued that the duties which attach to private life do not attach to citizens.﻿—I think it will be obvious to the eye of candor, that they are exceedingly indeterminate and vague. Little more appears to be done by Dr. Paley than to exhibit their doubtfulness. In truth, I do not know whether he has argued better in favor of his position, or against it. To me it appears that he has evinced it to be fallacious; for I do not think that *anything* can be a Christian truth, of which the truth cannot be more evidently proved. But whatever may be thought of the conclusion, the reader will certainly perceive that the whole question is involved in extreme vagueness and indecision: an indecision and vagueness, which it is difficult to conceive that Christianity ever intended should be hung over the very greatest question of practical morality that man has to determine; over the question that asks whether the followers of Christ are at liberty to destroy one another. That such a procedure as a war is, under any circumstances, sanctioned by Christianity, from whose principles it is acknowledged to be “abhorrent,” ought to be clearly made out. It ought to be obvious to loose examination. It ought not to be necessary to ascertaining it, that a critical investigation should be made, of questions which ordinary men cannot comprehend, and which, if they comprehended them, they could not determine; and above all, that investigation ought not to end, as we have seen it does end, in vague indecision﻿—in “doubts” of which even “Philosophy furnishes no precise solution.” But when this indecision and vagueness are brought to oppose the Christian evidence for peace; when it is contended, not only that it militates against that evidence, but that it outbalances and supersedes it﻿—we would say of such an argument, that it is not only weak, but idle; of such a conclusion, that it is not only unsound, but preposterous.

===Chinese===

以上便是据以主张属于私人生活的义务并不属于公民的权宜原则。——我相信在公正坦诚的目光审视下，显而易见，这些原则极其含混而模糊。佩利博士似乎除了展现其可疑性之外，并未多作证明。事实上，我不知道他在论证自己立场的正确性上更有力，还是在反驳该立场上更有力。在我看来，他恰恰证明了该立场的谬妄；因为我认为，任何无法被更显豁证明的事物，皆不可能成为基督教的真理。然而，无论对这一结论持何种看法，读者必然会察觉，整个问题都笼罩在极端的模糊与游移之中：很难想象，基督教竟会有意让此种游移与含混，笼罩在人类必须抉择的最重大的实践道德问题之上；笼罩在基督的追随者是否可以任意互相毁灭这一问题之上。战争这一行径若要在任何情形下获得据承认与其原则「格格不入」的基督教的认可，本应得到明白无误的论证。它本应经得起哪怕粗略的检视便一目了然。要确证这一点，绝不应当诉诸对常人无法理解、即便理解了也无力裁决的深奥问题的苛细探究；尤其是，正如我们所见，这种探究绝不应当以含混的动摇收场——以连「哲学都给不出确切解答」的「种种疑虑」告终。然而，当这种游移与含混被用来对抗基督教支持和平的明证；当人们不仅主张它削弱了那一明证，甚至主张它压倒并取代了那一明证时——对于这般论证，我们要说它不仅软弱无力，而且荒谬徒劳；对于这般结论，我们要说它不仅站不住脚，而且荒诞不经。

===Original===

Christian obligation is a much more simple thing that speculative philosophy would make it appear; and to all those who suppose that our relations as subjects dismiss the obligation of Christian laws, we would offer the consideration, that neither the Founder of Christianity nor his apostles ever made the distinction. Of questions of “particular and general consequences,” of “general advantages and particular mischiefs,” no traces are to be found in their words or writings. The morality of Christianity is a simple system, adapted to the comprehensions of ordinary men. Were it otherwise, what would be its usefulness? If philosophers only could examine our duties, and if their examinations ended in *doubts without solution*, how would men, without learning and without leisure, regulate their conduct? I think, indeed, that it is a sufficient objection to all such theories as the present, that they are not adapted to the wayfaring man. If the present theory be admitted, one of these two effects will be the consequence: the greater part of the community must trust for the discovery of their duties to the sagacity of others, or they must act without any knowledge of their duties at all.

But, that the pacific injunctions of the Christian Scriptures, do apply to us, under every circumstance of life, whether private or public, appears to be made necessary by the universality of Christian obligation. The language of Christianity upon the obligation of her moral laws, is essentially this﻿—“What I say unto you, I say unto all.” The pacific laws of our religion, then, are binding upon all men; upon the king and upon every individual who advises him, upon every member of a legislature, upon every officer and agent, and upon every private citizen. How then can *that* be lawful for a body of men which is unlawful for each individual? How, if one be disobedient, can his offence make disobedience lawful to all? We maintain yet more, and say, that to dismiss Christian benevolence as subjects, and to retain it as individuals, is simply impossible. He who possesses that subjugation of the affections and that universality of benevolence, by which he is influenced to do good to those who hate him, and to love his enemies in private life, cannot, without abandoning those dispositions, butcher other men because they are called public enemies.

===Chinese===

基督教的义务乃是一件远比思辨哲学所描绘的更为单纯之事；对于所有认定我们作为臣民的身份免除了遵行基督教法则之义务的人，我们愿提请其考量：无论是基督教的创立者还是他的使徒，皆从未作过此种界分。在他们的言论或著作中，根本寻觅不到任何关于「个别后果与一般后果」、关于「一般益处与个别祸患」等问题的踪迹。基督教的道德乃是一个朴素的体系，切合凡夫常人的理解能力。倘若不是如此，它又何用之有？倘若唯有哲学家才能探究我们的职责，而他们的探究又以无解的疑虑告终，那么既无学识又无闲暇的普通人，又当如何规制自己的行为？我确实认为，对当下一切此类理论最充分的反驳，莫过于它们并不切合尘世行路之人。倘若承认当下的理论，便必然导致以下两种后果之一：社群的大多数人必须仰赖他人的睿智来知晓自身的职责，抑或他们必须在对自身职责一无所知的情形下行事。

然而，无论处于私人生活还是公共生活的何种境遇之下，基督教圣经关于和平的训诫都必然适用于我们，这是由基督教义务的普遍性所决定的。基督教论及其道德法则之约束力时的言辞，实质上正是：「我对你们所说的话，也是对众人说。」因此，我们信仰中关于和平的法则，对所有人皆有约束力；对君王以及向他进言的每一个人、对立法机关的每一位成员、对每一位官员与执政人员、对每一位平民百姓，皆具约束力。那么，对每个个体而言皆为非法之事，怎能对群体而言反倒成了合法？倘若一人违命不从，他的罪衍又怎能使所有人的违命都成为合法？我们更进一步主张：作为臣民抛弃基督徒的仁爱，而作为个人予以保留，根本是绝无可能的。凡克制了私欲、心怀博大仁爱，因而在私人生活中善待恨他之人、爱其仇敌的人，若不彻底弃绝这些德性，便绝不可能仅仅因为他人被称为公敌，就去屠戮他们。

===Original===

The whole position, therefore, that the pacific commands and prohibitions of the Christian Scriptures do not apply to our conduct as subjects of a state, appears to me to be a fallacy. Some of the arguments which are brought to support it, so flippantly dispense with the principles of Christian obligation, so gratuitously assume, that because obedience may be difficult, obedience is not required, that they are rather an excuse for the distinction than a justification of it﻿—and some are so lamentably vague and indeterminate, the principles which are proposed are so technical, so inapplicable to the circumstance of society, and in truth, so incapable of being practically applied, that it is not credible that they were designed to suspend the obligation of rules which were imposed by a revelation from Heaven.

The reputation of Dr. Paley is so great, that, as he has devoted a chapter of the *Moral Philosophy* to “War and Military Establishments,” it will perhaps be expected, in an inquiry like the present, that some specific reference should be made to his opinions; and I make this reference willingly.

The chapter “On War” begins thus:﻿—“Because the Christian Scriptures describe wars, as what they are, as crimes or judgments, some men have been led to believe that it is unlawful for a Christian to bear arms. But it should be remembered, that it may be necessary for individuals to unite their force, and for this end to resign themselves to a common will; and yet it may be true that that will is often actuated by criminal motives, and often determined to destructive purposes.” This is a most remarkable paragraph: It assumes, at once, the whole subject of inquiry, and is an assumption couched in extraordinary laxity of language.﻿—“It may be necessary for individuals to unite their force.” The tea-table and the drawing-room have often told us this; but *philosophy* should tell us how the necessity is proved. Nor is the morality of the paragraph more rigid than the philosophy, “Wars are crimes,” and are often undertaken from “criminal motives, and determined to destructive purposes;” yet of these purposes, and motives, and crimes, “it may be necessary” for Christians to become the abettors and accomplices!

===Chinese===

因此，所谓基督教圣经中关于和平的诫命与禁令不适用于我们作为国家臣民之行为的整个立场，在我看来纯属谬论。那些被用来支持该立场的论点中，有些如此轻率地废弃基督教义务的原则，如此毫无根据地认定顺服既然艰难便无需顺服，以至于与其说它们是对这种界分的正当性证明，不如说是对它的托辞开脱——而另一些论点则是如此可悲地含混不清与游移不定，所提出的原则如此具有技术性、如此脱离社会实情，且在实际上如此无法付诸实践，以至于令人难以置信它们竟然旨在中止由上天启示所设立的准则之约束力。

佩利博士名望甚高，且在《道德哲学》（Moral Philosophy）中专门辟有一章讨论「战争与军事建置」，因此在当下这般探讨中，人们或许期望应对其见解作一番专门的论及；而我也欣然作出此番论及

「论战争」一章开篇如下：「因基督教圣经如实将战争描绘为罪行或天谴，有些人便认定基督徒执兵器乃是非法的。但应当记住的是，个人联合其力量，并为此顺从于一个共同意愿，或许是必要的；然而即便如此，该意愿往往受制于犯罪动机的驱使，且往往决意于破坏性的目的，这一点亦或属实。」这委实是一段极其引人注目的段落：它径直预设了整个探究的主题，并且这种预设是以格外松散含混的言辞表述出来的。——「个人联合其力量或许是必要的。」茶余饭后的闲谈与客厅里的寒暄早已屡屡告知我们这一点；但哲学应当告知我们的是这种必要性是如何得以证明的。该段落的道德考量也丝毫不比其哲学更为严谨：「战争乃是罪行」，且往往出于「犯罪动机，决意于破坏性目的」而发动；然而对于这些目的、动机与罪行，基督徒「或许有必要」成为其教唆者与共谋犯！

===Original===

Paley proceeds to say, that in the New Testament *the profession of a soldier*53 is nowhere forbidden or condemned; and he refers to the case of John the Baptist, of the Roman centurion, and of Cornelius; and with this he finishes all inquiry into the Christian evidence upon the subject, after having expended upon it less than a page of the edition before me.

These arguments are all derived from the silence of the New Testament, and to all reasoning founded upon this silence, no one can give a better answer than himself. In replying to the defences by which the advocates of slavery attempt to justify it, he notices that which they advance from *the silence of the New Testament* respecting it. He says﻿—It is urged that “Slavery was a part of the civil constitution of most countries when Christianity appeared; yet that no passage is to be found in the Christian Scriptures, by which it is condemned or prohibited.” “This,” he rejoins, “is true; for Christianity, soliciting admission into all nations of the world, abstained, as behooved it, from intermeddling with the civil institutions of any. But does it follow, from the silence of Scripture concerning them, that all the civil institutions which then prevailed were right, or that the bad should not be exchanged for better?” I beg the reader to apply this reasoning to Paley’s own arguments in favor of war from the silence of the Scriptures. How happens it that he did not remember it himself?

===Chinese===

佩利进而指出，在《新约》中，军人的职业 53 未在任何地方受到禁止或谴责；他援引了施洗约翰（John the Baptist）、罗马百夫长（Roman centurion）以及哥尼流（Cornelius）的事例；在我眼前的版本中，他在耗费了不到一页的篇幅之后，便以此终结了对该议题之基督教证据的全部探究

这些论点皆源自《新约》的沉默，而对于所有建立在此种沉默之上的推论，无人能给出比他本人更好的驳斥。在回应奴隶制辩护者企图为其正名所作的辩解时，他注意到了他们根据《新约》对此事的沉默所提出的论调。他写道——有人极力主张，「基督教诞生时，奴隶制乃是多数国家民事体制的一部分；然而在基督教圣经中，却找不到任何谴责或禁止奴隶制的经文。」「这确是事实，」他反驳道，「因为基督教谋求进入世间万邦，正如其理当做的那样，恪守克制而不干涉任何国家的民事建制。但难道能从圣经对这些建制的沉默中推导出，当时盛行的一切民事建制皆属正当，或者恶劣之物不应以良善之物加以替代吗？」我恳请读者将这段推论运用于佩利本人依据圣经之沉默而支持战争的论证中。他自己为何偏偏未能记起这番道理？

===Original===

Now I am compelled to observe, that in the discussion of the lawfulness of war, Dr. Paley has neglected his professed principles of decision and his ordinary practice. His professed principles are these: that the discovery of the “will of God, which is the whole business of morality,” is to be attained by referring, *primarily*, to “his express declarations when they are to be had, and which must be sought for in Scripture.”﻿—Has he sought for these declarations? Has he sought for “Resist not evil,” or for “Love your enemies,” or for “Put up thy sword,” or for “The weapons of our warfare are not carnal,” or for “My kingdom is not of this world?” He has sought for none of these; he has examined none of them. He has noticed none of them. His professed principles are, again, that *when our instructions are dubious, we should endeavor to explain them by what we can collect of our Master’s general inclination or intention*.54 Has he conformed to his own rule? Has he endeavored to collect this general inclination, and to examine this general tendency? He has taken no notice of it whatever. This neglect, we say, is contrary to his ordinary practice. Upon other subjects, he has assiduously applied to the Christian Scriptures in determination of truth. He has examined not only their direct evidence, but the evidence which they afford by induction and implication﻿—the evidence arising from their general tendency. Suicide is nowhere condemned in the New Testament, yet Paley condemns it, and how? He examines the sacred volume, and finds that by implication and inference, it may be collected that suicide is not permitted by Christianity. He says that patience under suffering is inculcated as an important duty; and that the recommendation of patience, implies the unlawfulness of suicide to get out of suffering. This is sound reasoning, but he does not adopt it in the examination of war. Could he not have found that the inculcation of peaceableness forms as good an argument against the lawfulness of war, as the inculcation of patience forms against the lawfulness of suicide? He certainly could have done this, and why has he not done it? Why has he passed it over in silence?

===Chinese===

如今我不得不指出，在探讨战争的正当性时，佩利博士背离了他公开宣称的决断原则和他一贯的治学实践。他公开宣称的原则是：探寻「上帝的旨意，即道德的全部要务」，首先必须诉诸「他在圣经中显明且可资查考的明确昭示」。——他是否查考了这些昭示？他是否查考过「不要与恶人作对」，或「要爱你们的仇敌」，或「收刀入鞘吧」，或「我们争战的兵器本不是属血气的」，或「我的国不属这世界」？他一项也没有查考；他一项也没有审视；他一项也没有提及。此外，他公开宣称的原则还包括：当所领受的教训含混不清时，我们应当尽力根据从主的一般倾向或意图中搜集到的认识来予以阐明。54 他遵从了自己的法则吗？他是否竭力去领会这一普遍意向、审视这一普遍倾向？他根本未予丝毫理会。我们认为，这种疏忽与其一贯的实践截然相反。在其他议题上，他总是孜孜不倦地求索于基督教圣经以断定真理。他不仅审视其直接证据，而且审视经文通过归纳与含蓄暗示所提供的证据——即源于其总体倾向的证据。自杀在《新约》中未在任何地方受到谴责，然而佩利却予以谴责，他是如何做到的？他研读这卷圣典，发现通过暗示与推论，可以断定基督教绝不容许自杀。他指出，在苦难中忍耐被谆谆教导为一项重要职责；而对忍耐的倡导，便含蓄昭示了以自杀逃避苦难乃是非法的。这确是合理的推论，但他却未将其运用于对战争的审视之中。难道他不能发现，对和平敦睦的谆谆教导，正如对忍耐的教导足以构成反对自杀正当性的有力论据一样，同样构成了反对战争正当性的有力论据吗？他本完全可以做到这一点，但他为何不这样做？他为何对此保持沉默？

===Original===

I must confess my belief, that he was unwilling to discuss the subject upon Christian principles; that he had resolved to make war consistent with Christianity; and that, foreseeing her “express declarations” and “general intentions” militated against it, he avoided noticing them at all. Thus much at least is certain, that in discussing the lawfulness of war, he has abandoned both his avowed principles and his correspondent practice. There is, to me at least, in the chapter “On War,” an appearance of great indecision of mind, arising from the conflict between Christian truth and the power of habit﻿—between the consciousness that war is “abhorrent” to our religion, and the desire to defend it on the principle of expediency. The whole chapter is characterized by a very extraordinary laxity both of arguments and principles.

After the defensibility of war has been proved, or assumed, in the manner which we have exhibited, Dr. Paley states the occasions upon which he determines that wars become justifiable. “The objects of *just* wars,” says he, “are precaution, defence, or reparation.”﻿—“Every *just* war supposes an injury perpetrated, attempted or feared.”

===Chinese===

我必须坦陈我的信念：他根本不愿依据基督教原则来探讨这一议题；他早已决意要使战争与基督教相契合；并且他预见到基督教的「明确昭示」与「普遍意向」皆与战争相抵触，因而索性避而不谈。至少有一点是确凿无疑的：在探讨战争的正当性时，他背弃了自己公开宣示的原则以及与其相符的治学实践。至少在我看来，在「论战争」一章中显露出了极大的思想动摇，这源于基督教真理与习俗力量之间的冲突——源于深知战争与我们的信仰「格格不入」的清醒意识，同依据权宜原则为之辩护的欲求之间的冲突。论据与原则上非同寻常的极度松散，乃是贯穿该章全篇的显著特征。

在以我们所展示的方式证明或预设了战争的合辩护性之后，佩利博士阐述了他判定战争变得具有正当性的种种时机。「正义战争的目的，」他写道，「在于防范、防御或赔偿。」——「每一场正义战争，皆以一种已被实施、企图实施或令人恐惧的伤害为前提。」

===Original===

I shall acknowledge, that if these be justifying motives to war, I see very little purpose in talking of morality upon the subject. It was wise to leave the principles of Christianity out of the question, and to pass them by unnoticed, if they were to be succeeded by principles like these. It is vain to expatiate on moral obligations, if we are at liberty to declare war whenever an “injury is feared.” An injury, without limit to its insignificance! A fear without stipulation for its reasonableness! The judges, also, of the reasonableness of fear, are to be they who are under its influence; and who so likely to judge amiss as those who are afraid? Sounder philosophy than this has told us, that “he who has to reason upon his duty when the temptation to transgress it is before him, is almost sure to reason himself into an error.” The necessity for this ill-timed reasoning, and the allowance of it, is amongst the capital objections to the philosophy of Paley. It tells us that a people may suspend the laws of God when they think it is “expedient;” and they are to judge of this expediency when the temptation to transgression is before them!﻿—Has Christianity left the lawfulness of human destruction to be determined on such principles as these?

===Chinese===

我必须承认，如果这些都可以成为战争的正当动机，那么在此议题上谈论道德便毫无意义。若基督教的原则终将被此类原则所取代，那么将它们置于讨论之外、视若无睹，倒不失为明智之举。如果只要「担忧遭受侵害」便可随意宣战，那么大肆阐发道德义务便纯属徒劳。侵害，竟对其微不足道毫无限制！恐惧，竟对其是否合理毫无规范！而且，恐惧是否合理的裁判者，偏偏正是那些身受其影响的人；而除了心怀恐惧之人，又有谁更容易做出谬判呢？比这更健全的哲学早已告诫我们：「凡在违背义务的诱惑摆在面前时才去推敲自身义务的人，几乎必然会通过自辩陷入谬误。」这种不合时宜的推敲之必要性以及对它的容许，正是人们对佩利哲学的主要反驳之一。它告诉我们，一国国民若自认为「符合权宜」，便可中止上帝的律法；而裁定这一权宜与否的时刻，偏偏正是违背律法的诱惑摆在他们面前之时！——难道基督教竟会将毁灭人类的合法性，交由此类原则来裁断吗？

===Original===

Violence, rapine, and ambition, are not to be restrained by morality like this. It may serve for the speculation of a study; but we will venture to affirm that mankind will never be controlled by it. Moral rules are useless, if, from their own nature, they cannot be, or will not be applied. Who believes that if kings and conquerors may fight when they have fears, they will not fight when they have them not? The morality allows too much latitude to the passions to retain any practical restraint upon them. And a morality that will not be practised, I had almost said, that cannot be practised, is an useless morality. It is a *theory* of morals. We want clearer and more exclusive rules; we want more obvious and immediate sanctions. It were in vain for a philosopher to say to a general who was burning for glory, “You are at liberty to engage in the war, provided you have suffered, or fear you will suffer an injury; otherwise Christianity prohibits it.” He will tell him of twenty injuries that have been suffered, of a hundred that have been attempted, and of ten thousand that he fears. And what answer can the philosopher make to him?

===Chinese===

暴力、劫掠与野心，绝非此类道德所能遏制。它或可充作书斋里的沉思玄想；但我们敢断言，人类绝不可能受其管束。道德法则若因其自身本性而无法或不会被付诸应用，便毫无用处。若君王与征服者在心怀恐惧时便可诉诸交战，谁又会相信他们在毫无恐惧时便不会开战？这种道德给予了激情过大的放任余地，以致无法对激情施加任何切实约束。而一种不会被践行——我几乎要说，一种无法被践行的道德——乃是无用的道德。它不过是一套道德「理论」而已。我们需要更明确、更具排他性的规则；我们需要更昭著、更直接的制裁。哲学家若对一位渴求荣耀的将军说：「只要你曾遭受侵害，或担忧将遭受侵害，你便可随意投入战争；否则，基督教是严加禁止的。」这纯属枉费唇舌。那位将军自会向他列举出二十起已遭受的侵害、一百起企图施加的侵害，以及一万起他所担忧的侵害。届时，哲学家又能给予他怎样的答复呢？

===Original===

I think that Dr. Paley has, in another and a later work, given us stronger arguments in favor of peace than the *Moral Philosophy* gives in favor of war. In the *Evidences of Christianity* we find these statements:﻿—“The two following positions appear to me to be satisfactorily made out: first, That the gospel *omits some qualities*, which have usually engaged the praises and admiration of mankind, but which, in reality, and in their general effects, have been *prejudicial to human happiness*; secondly, that the gospel has *brought forward some virtues*, which *possess the highest intrinsic value*, but which have commonly been overlooked and condemned.﻿—The second of these propositions is exemplified in the instances of passive courage or endurance of suffering, patience under affronts and injuries, humility, irresistance, placability.﻿—The truth is, there are two opposite descriptions of character under which mankind may be generally classed. The one possesses vigor, firmness, resolution, is daring and active, quick in its sensibilities, jealous in its fame, eager in its attachments, inflexible in its purpose, violent in its resentments. The other meek, yielding, complying, forgiving, not prompt to act, but willing to suffer, silent and gentle under rudeness and insult, suing for reconciliation where others would demand satisfaction, giving way to the pushes of imprudence, conceding and indulgent to the prejudices, the wrong-headedness, the intractability of those with whom it has to deal.﻿—The former of these character is, and ever hath been, the favorite of the world.﻿—Yet so it hath happened, that with the Founder of Christianity, *this latter is the subject of his commendation, his precepts, his example*; and that *the former is so, in no part of its composition*. This morality shows, at least, that *no two things can be more different than the heroic and the Christian characters*. Now it is proved, in contradiction to first impressions, to popular opinion, to the encomiums of orators and poets, and even to the suffrages of historians and moralists, that *the latter character possesses most of true worth*, both as being most difficult either to be acquired or sustained, and as *contributing most to the happiness and tranquillity of social life*.

===Chinese===

我认为，佩利博士在其另一部较晚的著作中，为和平所提供的论据，比他在《道德哲学》中为战争所作的辩护更为有力。在《基督教的证据》中，我们读到如下论述：「在我看来，以下两个命题已得到令人信服的阐明：其一，福音书略去了某些通常赢得人类赞美与钦佩的品质，而这些品质在现实中及其总体影响上，实则有损于人类的幸福；其二，福音书提出了一些具备最高内在价值的美德，而这些美德通常却被忽视与贬抑。——这第二个命题体现在被动勇气或隐忍苦难、面对侮辱与伤害时的坚忍、谦卑、不抵抗以及宽和乐释的例证之中。——事实在于，人类大致可归入两种截然对立的品格范畴。其一富有活力、坚定、果决，勇敢而积极，感触敏锐，珍惜声誉，情感炽烈，意志坚决，怨恨强烈。另一种则温良、退让、顺从、宽宥，不急于行动而甘愿承受，面对粗暴与羞辱沉默温和，在他人要求决斗雪耻之处谋求和解，在鲁莽相逼面前退避三舍，对与之交往之人的偏见、执拗与乖戾予以容让与体恤。——前一种品格乃是世人的宠儿，自古皆然。——然而事情恰恰在于，在基督教创立者那里，后一种品格才是其赞赏、其训诫、其榜样的主旨所在；而前一种品格在其体系的任何部分皆无立足之地。这种道德至少表明，世间绝无任何两样事物比英武品格与基督徒品格更为截然对立。如今业已证明，与直觉印象、世俗舆论、演说家与诗人的赞歌乃至历史学家和道德学家的拥护截然相反，后一种品格具有最充盈的真正价值，这不仅因为它最难以养成与维系，更在于它对社会生活的幸福与安宁贡献最大。

===Original===

﻿—If this disposition were universal, the case is clear; the world would be a society of friends: whereas, if the other disposition were universal, it would produce a scene of universal contention. The world would not be able to hold a generation of such men. If, what is the fact, the disposition be partial; if a few be actuated by it amongst a multitude who are not, *in whatever degree it does prevail it prevents, allays, and terminates quarrels, the great disturbers of human happiness, and the great sources of human misery*, so far as man’s happiness and misery depend upon man. *The preference of the patient to the heroic character*, which we have here noticed, is a peculiarity in the Christian institution, which I propose as *an argument of wisdom*.”55

===Chinese===

——倘若这种性情普及全人类，事情便昭然若揭；整个世界将成为一个融洽无间的朋友社会：反之，若另一种性情普及开来，则必将造成普遍纷争的惨象。世界将无法容纳这样一代人。如果像现实中那样，这种性情仅存在于部分人心中；如果在未具此性情的芸芸众生中仅有少数人受其驱策，那么在人类的幸福与苦难取决于人类自身的限度内，无论这种性情在何种程度上得以盛行，它都能预防、平息并终止纷争——纷争正是人类幸福的大敌，亦是人类苦难的渊薮。我们在此注意到的、对于坚忍品格相较于英武品格的推崇，乃是基督教制度的一大特质，我谨将其作为展现其智慧的一大论据。」55

===Original===

These are the sentiments of Dr. Paley upon this great characteristic of the Christian morality. I think that in their plain, literal, and unsophisticated meaning, they exclude the possibility of the lawfulness of war. The simple conclusion from them is, that violence, and devastation, and human destruction cannot exist in conjunction with the character of a Christian. This would be the conclusion of the inhabitant of some far and peaceful island, where war and Christianity were alike unknown. If he read these definitions of the Christian duties, and were afterwards told that we thought ourselves allowed to plunder and to murder one another, he would start in amazement at the monstrous inconsistency. Casuistry may make her “distinctions,” and philosophy may talk of her “expediencies,” but the monstrous inconsistency remains. What is the fact? Muhammadans and Pagans do not believe that our religion allows of war. They reproach us with the inconsistency. Our wars are, with them, a scandal and a taunt. “You preach to us,” say they, “of Christianity, and would convert us to your creed;﻿—first convert yourselves; show us that yourselves believe in it.” Nay, the Jews at our own doors, tell us, that our wars are an evidence that the Prince of Peace is not come. They bring the violence of Christians to prove that Christ was a deceiver. Thus do we cause the way of truth to be evil spoken of. Thus, are we, who should be the helpers of the world, its stumbling-blocks and its shame. We, who should be lights to them that sit in darkness, cause them to love that darkness still. Well may the Christian be ashamed of these things: Well may he be ashamed for the reputation of his religion: And he may be ashamed too, for the honored defender of the Christian faith who stands up, the advocate of blood; who subtilizes the sophisms of the schools, and roves over the fields of speculation to find an argument to convince us that we may murder one another! This is the “wisdom of the world;” that wisdom which is, emphatically, “**foolishness**.”

===Chinese===

以上便是佩利博士对基督教道德这一伟大特质的见解。我认为，就其质朴、字面且未加曲解的原意而言，这些论述彻底排除了战争合法的可能性。从中得出的直接结论是：暴力、蹂躏与毁灭同胞，绝不可能与基督徒的品格并存。这也会是某个遥远而和平之岛屿上居民的结论，在那里，战争与基督教同样鲜为人知。倘若他读到这些关于基督徒义务的界定，随后却被告知我们自认为可以彼此抢掠与残杀，他定会因这骇人听闻的自相矛盾而惊愕万分。决疑论尽可做出种种「区分」，哲学亦可大谈其「权宜之计」，但那荒谬绝伦的自相矛盾依然存在。事实究竟如何？穆斯林与异教徒根本不相信我们的宗教容许战争。他们指责我们的言行相悖。在我们这里发生的战争，在他们眼中乃是丑闻与笑柄。他们说：「你们向我们宣讲基督教，想让我们皈依你们的信仰；——请先让你们自己归信吧；先向我们证明你们自己真正笃信它。」不仅如此，就在我们身旁的犹太人也对我们说，我们的战争正证明了和平之君尚未降临。他们援引基督徒的暴力行径，来证明基督不过是个骗子。我们竟如此使得真道受人毁谤。我们本应成为世人的助佑，如今却成了世人的绊脚石与耻辱。我们本应成为照亮坐在黑暗中之人的明光，却反而致使他们继续留恋那份黑暗。基督徒理当为这些事深感羞愧：他理当为其宗教的名声深感羞愧；他也理当为那位受人尊崇的基督教信仰辩护士深感羞愧——此人竟然挺身而出，充当流血的代言人；他把经院式的诡辩推向极致，在思辨的旷野中四处搜寻，只为找到一条论据来说服我们：我们彼此残杀是正当的！这就是「世上的智慧」；而那等智慧，千真万确正是「愚拙」。

===Original===

We have seen that the principle on which Dr. Paley’s *Moral Philosophy* decides that war is lawful, is, that it is expedient. I know not how this argument accords with some of the statements of the *Evidences of Christianity*. We are there told that the non-resisting character possesses “the highest intrinsic value,” and the “most of true worth;” that it “prevents the great disturbances of human happiness,” and destroys “the great sources of human misery,” and that it “contributes most to the happiness and tranquillity of social life.” And in what then does expediency consist, if the non-resisting character be not expedient? Dr. Paley says, again, in relation to the immense mischief and bloodshed arising from the violation of Christian duty﻿—“We do not say that no evil can exceed this, nor any possible advantage compensate it, but we say that a loss which affects all, *will scarcely be made up to the common stock of human happiness, by any benefit that can be procured to a single nation*.” And is not therefore the violation of the duty *inexpedient* as well as criminal? He says again that the warlike character “*is, in its general effects, prejudicial to human happiness*,”﻿—and, therefore, surely, it is inexpedient.

===Chinese===

我们已经看到，佩利博士在《道德哲学》中断定战争合法的原则，在于战争符合权宜。我不知道这一论点如何能与他在《基督教的证据》中的若干论述相融洽。我们在后者中读到，不抵抗的品格具备「最高的内在价值」与「最充盈的真正价值」；它「预防了对人类幸福的重大破坏」，根除了「人类苦难的重大渊薮」，并且「对社会生活的幸福与安宁贡献最大」。如果连不抵抗的品格都不符合权宜，那么权宜究竟体现在何处？针对违背基督徒义务所带来的巨大祸患与流血牺牲，佩利博士又说道：「我们并非说没有任何祸恶能超过此端，亦非说任何可能的好处都无法补偿它；但我们断言，波及万民的损失，绝难由带给单一国家的任何利益弥补到人类幸福的共同总和之中。」如此看来，违背这一义务岂非既是犯罪，亦完全违背权宜吗？他再度指出，尚武的品格「在其普遍影响上，实则有损于人类的幸福」——既然如此，它显然是不符合权宜的。

===Original===

The advocate of war, in the abundance of his topics of defence (or in the penury of them) has had recourse to this:﻿—*That as a greater number of male children are brought into the world than of female, wars are the ordination of Providence to rectify the inequality*; and one or two moralists have proceeded a step farther, and have told us, not that war is designed to carry off the excess, but *that an excess is born in order to supply its slaughters*. Dreadful! Are we told that God sends too many of his rational creatures into the world, and therefore that He stands in need of wars to destroy them? Has He no other means of adjusting the proportions of the species, than by a system which violates the revelation that He has made, and the duties that He has imposed? Or, yet more dreadful﻿—are we to be told that He creates an excess of one of the sexes, on purpose that their destruction of each other may be with impunity to the species? This reasoning surely is sufficiently confident:﻿—I fear it is more than sufficiently profane. But alas for the argument! It happens most unfortunately for it, that although more males are born than females, yet from the greater mortality of the former, it is found that long before the race arrives at maturity, the number of females predominates. What a pity﻿—that just as the young men had grown old enough to kill one another, it should be discovered that there are not too many to remain peacefully alive! Let them, the principle be retained and acted upon; and since we have no an excess of females, let us send forth an armament of women, that their redundance may be lopped by the appointed means.﻿—But really it is time for the defender of war to abandon reasoning like this. It argues little in favor of any cause, that its advocates have resource to such deplorable subterfuges.

The magistrate “beareth not the sword in vain; for he is the minister of God, a revenger to execute wrath upon him that doeth evil.” From this acknowledgment of the lawfulness of coercion on the part of the civil magistrate, an argument has been advanced in favor of war. It is said, that by parity of reasoning, coercion is also lawful in the suppression of the violence which one nation uses towards another.

===Chinese===

战争的辩护者，在其辩护论据的充沛（抑或匮乏）之中，竟然诉诸了这样一种说辞：降生人世的男婴数量多于女婴，因此战争乃是天意的安排，旨在纠偏这一不均；有一两名道德学家甚至更进了一步，他们告诉我们，并非战争被设计用来清除过剩人口，而是过剩之人的降生就是为了供应战争的屠杀。何等骇人听闻！难道我们被告知，上帝差遣了过多的理性造物来到世间，以至于祂需要借由战争来摧毁他们吗？难道祂调节物种比例的手段，除了通过一套践踏祂所赐启示、背弃祂所定职责的体制之外，便别无他途了吗？抑或——更为可怖的是——难道我们竟然要接受，祂特意创造了某一性别的过剩，就是为了让他们自相残杀而不致危害整个物种的延续吗？这种推论固然自信满满，我却深恐它充斥着无可复加的亵渎。然而，这套论点何其可悲！对该论调极为不利的是，尽管出生的男性多于女性，但由于前者的死亡率更高，人们发现，早在这一族群步入成年之前，女性的数量便已占据优势。何其讽刺——正当青年男子长到足以自相残杀的年纪时，人们却赫然发现，要在和平中存活于世，他们的人数根本算不上过剩！那么，就请保留并践行这一原则吧；既然如今女性过剩，就让我们派出一支由妇女组成的大军，以便用既定的手段来削减其多余的人口吧。——然而，战争的辩护者实在该摒弃此类狡辩了。其倡导者竟然沦落到诉诸如此可怜的托辞，这对于任何事业的正当性而言都毫无助益。

执政长官「并不空空地佩剑；因为他是神的用人，是伸冤的，刑罚那作恶的」。基于对世俗执政官行使强制力之合法性的这一承认，有人提出了一项支持战争的论证。他们声称，依同理推导，在遏制一国对另一国所施加的暴力时，强制手段同样合法。

===Original===

Some men talk as if the principles which we maintain were subversive of all order and government. They ask us﻿—Is the civil magistrate to stand still and see lawless violence ravaging the land? Is the whole fabric of human society to be dissolved? We answer, No; and that whencesoever these men may have derived their terrors, they are not chargeable upon us or upon our principles. To deduce even a plausible argument in favor of war from the permission “to execute wrath upon him that doeth evil,” it is obviously necessary to show that we are permitted to take his life. And the right to put an offender to death, must be proved, if it can be proved at all, either *from an express permission of the Christian Scriptures*, or, supposing Christianity to have given no decisions, either directly or indirectly, *from a necessity which knows no alternative*. Now everyone knows that this express permission to inflict death is not to be found; and, upon the question of its *necessity*, we ask for that evidence which alone can determine it﻿—the evidence of experience: and this evidence, the advocate of war, has never brought, and cannot bring. And we shall probably not be contradicted when we say, that that degree of evidence which experience has afforded, is an evidence in our favor rather than against us.

But some persons entertain an opinion, that in the case of murder, at least, there is a sort of immutable necessity for taking the offender’s life. “Whoso sheddeth man’s blood, by man shall his blood be shed.” If anyone urges this rule against us, we reply, that it is not a rule of Christianity; and if the necessity of demanding blood for blood is an everlasting principle of retributive justice, how happens it that, in the first case in which murder was committed, the murderer was not put to death?

===Chinese===

有人言之凿凿，仿佛我们所持守的原则会颠覆一切秩序与政体。他们质问我们——难道执政长官应当袖手旁观，听任无法无天的暴力蹂躏国土吗？难道整个人类社会的结构注定要土崩瓦解吗？我们回答说：绝非如此；无论这些人从何处引申出他们的恐慌，这都不能归咎于我们，亦不能归咎于我们的原则。若要从「刑罚那作恶的」这一许可中，推导出一个看似合情合理的挺战论据，显然必须先证明我们被准许剥夺作恶者的生命。而将罪犯处死的权利，若果真能够被证实，其证明途径要么来自基督教圣经的明文准许，要么（假定基督教未曾直接或间接作出裁决）来自某种别无选择的必然性。然而众所周知，判处死刑的明文准许在圣经中根本无从寻觅；至于其必然性的问题，我们所寻求的乃是唯一能对此作出决断的明证——即经验的明证：而战争的辩护者从未提出过、亦绝不可能提出此类明证。当我们断言经验所能提供的证据皆有利于我们而非不利于我们时，大概不会招致什么反驳。

然而，某些人持有这样一种看法：至少在谋杀案中，处死罪犯存在着某种亘古不变的必然性。「凡流人血的，他的血也必被人所流。」若有人以此法则来责难我们，我们的答复是：这绝非基督教的法则；况且，倘若以血偿血的必然性果真是报应正义的永恒原则，那么在人类发生的第一起谋杀案中，凶手为何未被处死呢？

===Original===

The philosopher, however, would prove what the Christian cannot; and Mably accordingly says, “In the state of nature, I have a right to take the life of him who lifts his arm against mine. *This right, upon entering into society, I surrender to the magistrate.*” If we conceded the truth of the first position (which we do not,) the conclusion from it is a sophism too idle for notice. Having, however, been thus told that the state has a right to kill, we are next informed, by Filangieri, that the criminal has no right to live. He says, “If I have a right to kill another man, *he has lost his right to life*.”56 Rousseau goes a little farther. He tells us, that in consequence of the “social contract” which we make with the sovereign on entering into society, “Life is a conditional grant of the state:”57 so that we hold our lives, it seems, only as “tenants at will,” and must give them up whenever their owner, the state, requires them. The reader has probably hitherto thought that he retained his head by some other tenure.

The right of taking an offender’s life being thus proved, Mably shows us how its exercise becomes expedient. “A murderer,” says he, “in taking away his enemy’s life, *believes that he does him the greatest possible evil*. Death, then, in the murderer’s estimation, is the greatest of evils. *By the fear of death, therefore*, the excesses of hatred and revenge must be restrained.” If language wilder than this can be held, Rousseau, I think, holds it. He says, “The preservation of both sides (the criminal and the state) is incompatible; one of the two must perish.” How it happens that a nation “must perish,” if a convict is not hanged, the reader, I suppose, will not know.

===Chinese===

然而，哲学家企图证明基督徒所无法证明之事；马布利（Mably）于是声称：「在自然状态下，对于向我举臂相向之人，我有权取其性命。而一经进入社会，我便将这项权利让渡给了执政官。」即便我们退让一步承认前一个前提属实（实则我们绝不承认），由此得出的推论也是一种荒唐得不值一顾的诡辩。然而，在被人如此告知国家享有杀人权之后，费朗吉埃里（Filangieri）接着又告知我们罪犯不再享有生存的权利。他说：「如果我有权杀死另一个人，他就丧失了生命权。」56 卢梭（Rousseau）则走得更远。他宣称，由于我们在进入社会时与主权者缔结了「社会契约」，「生命乃是国家附带条件的恩赐」：57 如此看来，我们对自己生命的维系，仿佛仅相当于「任意承租人」，只要其真正所有者——国家——有所要求，我们就必须随时交出生命。读者在此之前大概一直以为，自己保全项上人头是依据某种更为坚实的占有权吧。

剥夺罪犯生命的权利既已被这般证明，马布利便向我们展示行使这一权利如何符合权宜。「一个凶手，」他写道，「在夺取仇敌的性命时，自以为施加了可能的最大祸患。因此，在凶手的评估中，死亡乃是最大的祸恶。是以，唯有借由对死亡的畏惧，仇恨与报复的肆虐才能够受到遏制。」倘若世间还能有比这更为荒诞狂妄的言论，我认为便是卢梭的高论了。他说：「双方（罪犯与国家）的保存是不可调和的；两者之中必有一方灭亡。」倘若一个囚犯未被绞死，一个国家究竟何以「必定灭亡」，我想读者对此大概同样茫然不知。

===Original===

I have referred to these speculations for the purpose of showing, that the right of putting offenders to death is not easily made out. Philosophers would scarcely have had recourse to the metaphysical abstractions if they knew an easier method of establishing the right. Even philosophy, however, concedes us much:﻿—“*Absolute necessity, alone*,” says Pastoret, “can justify the punishment of death;” and Rousseau himself acknowledges, that, “we have no right to put to death, *even for the sake of example*, any but those who cannot be permitted to live without danger.” Beccaria limits the right to two specific cases; in which, “if an individual, though deprived of his liberty, has still such credit and connections as may endanger the security of the nation, or, by his existence, is likely to produce a dangerous revolution in the established form of government﻿—he must undoubtedly die.”58 It is not, perhaps, necessary for us to point out why, in these suppositious cases, a prisoner may not be put to death; since I believe that philosophy will find it difficult, on some of her own principles, to justify his destruction: For Dr. Paley decides, that whenever a man thinks there are great grievances in the existing government, and that, by heading a revolt, he can redress them, without occasioning greater evil by the rebellion than benefit by its success﻿—*it is his duty to rebel*.59 The prisoner whom Beccaria supposes, may be presumed to have thought this; and with reason too, for the extent of his credit, his connections, and his success, is the plea for putting him to death; and we must therefore, leave it to those who indulge in such speculations, to consider how it can be right for one may to take the lead in a revolution, whilst it is right for another to hang him for taking it.

What then, does the lawfulness of coercion on the part of the magistrate prove upon the question of the lawfulness of war? If capital punishments *had never been inflicted*, what would it have proved? Obviously nothing. If capital punishments *cannot be shown to be defensible*, what does it prove? Obviously nothing: for an unauthorized destruction of human life on the gallows, cannot justify another unauthorized destruction of it on the field.

===Chinese===

我援引这些思辨，旨在表明处死罪犯的权利绝非轻易便能确立。哲学家若通晓某种更为简易的方法来确立这项权利，便断不会诉诸形而上学的抽象概念。然而，即便是哲学，也向我们做出了极大的退让：帕斯托雷（Pastoret）言道，「唯有绝对的必然性，才能证明死刑的正当性」；而卢梭本人亦承认，「即便为了以儆效尤，除了那些若容其活命便无法消除危险的人之外，我们无权处死任何人」。贝卡里亚（Beccaria）将这一权利严格限制在两种特定情形之内；在这两种情形中，「一个人虽被剥夺自由，却依然拥有可能危及国家安全的崇高威信与广泛人脉，抑或其存在很可能在既定政体中引发一场危险的革命——那么他毫无疑问必须死」。58 我们或许无需指出为何在这些假想的情形中不能将囚犯处死；因为我相信，哲学依据其自身的部分原则，便很难为其处死辩护：盖因佩利博士断定，每当有人认为现有政体中存在严重弊政，且深信自己带头起义便可纠正这些弊端，同时叛乱所招致的祸害不会大于其成事带来的福祉时——反叛便是他的职责。59 贝卡里亚所假定的那名囚徒，大体也可以被推测为持有此等想法，且其理由相当充分，因为其威望之广、人脉之深及其成功的概率，恰恰成了置其于死地的借口；因此，我们只能留待那些沉溺于此类推论的人去深思：一个人带头起义何以是正当的，而另一个人因他带头起义而将他送上绞架又何以是正当的。

那么，执政长官强制力的合法性，在战争合法性的问题上究竟能证明什么？倘若死刑从不曾被施加过，它又能证明什么？显然什么都证明不了。倘若死刑无法被证明具有正当性，它又能证明什么？显然什么都证明不了：因为在绞刑架上对人类生命的擅自剥夺，断不能为在沙场上对生命的另一场擅自剥夺提供正当辩护。

===Original===

Perhaps some of those who may have been hitherto willing to give me a patient attention, will be disposed to withdraw it, when they hear the unlawfulness of defensive war unequivocally maintained. But it matters not. My business is with what appears to me to be truth: if truth surprises us, I cannot help it﻿—still it is truth.

Upon the question of defensive war, I would beg the reader to bear in his recollection, that every feeling of his nature is enlisted against us; and I would beg him, knowing this, to attain as complete an abstraction from the influence of those feelings as shall be in his power. This he will do, if he is honest in the inquiry for truth. It is not necessary to conceal that the principles which we maintain may sometimes demand the sacrifice of our apparent interests. Such sacrifices Christianity has been wont to require: they are the tests of our fidelity; and of those whom I address, I believe there are some, who, if they can be assured that we speak the language of Christianity, will require no other inducements to obedience.

The lawfulness of defensive war is commonly simplified to *The Right of Self-defence*. This is one of the strongholds of the defender of war, the almost final fastness to which he retires. *The instinct of self-preservation*, it is said, *is an instinct of nature; and since this instinct is implanted by God, whatever is necessary to self-preservation is accordant with his will*. This is specious, but like many other specious arguments, it is sound in its premises, but, as I think, fallacious in its conclusions. That the instinct of self-preservation is an instinct of nature, is clear﻿—that, because it is an instinct of nature, we have a right to kill other men, is *not* clear.

===Chinese===

迄今为止或许一直愿意耐心倾听我论述的读者中，当听闻自卫战争的不法性被明确维护时，有些人可能会决意收回这份耐心。但这无关紧要。我的职责乃是探求在我看来属于真理之事：倘若真理令我们惊诧，我也无可奈何——它终究是真理。

关于自卫战争的问题，我恳请读者谨记，他天性中的每一种情感都已被动员起来与我们作对；我亦恳请他，在深知这一点的同时，竭尽全力摆脱这些情感的影响，达到尽可能彻底的超然。倘若他在探求真理时秉持真诚，他便会这样做。毋庸隐瞒的是，我们所坚持的原则有时可能会要求牺牲我们显而易见的利益。基督教历来就要求做出此类牺牲：它们是对我们忠诚的检验；而在我所倾诉的读者当中，我相信定然有人，只要确信我们所言符合基督教的精神，便无需其他诱因而甘心顺服。

自卫战争的正当性，通常被简化为「自卫的权利」。这是战争辩护者的坚固据点之一，几乎是他退守的最后堡垒。论者声称：「自我保存的本能乃是自然的天性；既然这种本能是上帝所植入的，那么凡对自我保存所必需之举，皆符合他的旨意。」这种说法看似言之成理，但如同许多其他似是而非的论辩一样，其前提固然成立，而在我看来，其结论却是谬误的。自我保存的本能属于自然天性，这一点昭然若揭——但若说正因它是自然天性，我们便有权杀戮他人，这一点却绝非昭然。

===Original===

The fallacy of the whole argument appears to consist in this﻿—that it assumes that an instinct of nature is a law of *paramount* authority. God has implanted in the human system various propensities or instincts, of which the purposes are wise. These propensities tend in their own nature to *abuse*; and when gratified or followed to excess, they become subversive of the purposes of the wisdom which implanted them, and destructive of the welfare of mankind. He has therefore instituted a *superior law*, sanctioned by his immediate authority: by this law, we are required to regulate these propensities. The question therefore is, not whether the instinct of self-preservation is implanted by nature, but whether Christianity has restricted its operation. By this, and by this only, the question is to be determined. Now he who will be at the trouble of making the inquiry, will find that a regulation of the instincts of nature, and a restriction of their exercise, is a prominent object of the Christian morality; and I think it is plain that this regulation and restriction apply to the instinct before us. That some of these propensities are to be restrained is certain. One of the most powerful instincts of our nature, is an affection to which the regulating precepts of Christianity are peculiarly directed. I do not maintain that any natural instinct is to be eradicated, but that all of them are to be regulated and restrained; and I maintain this of the instinct of self-preservation.

The establishment of this position is, indeed, the great object of the present inquiry. What are the dispositions and actions to which the instinct of self-preservation prompts, but actions and dispositions which Christianity forbids? They are non-forbearance, resistance, retaliation of injuries. The truth is, that it is to *defence* that the peaceable precepts of Christianity are directed. *Offence* appears not to have even suggested itself. It is “Resist not *evil*;” it is “Overcome *evil* with good;” it is “Do good to them that hate you;” it is “Love your *enemies*;” it is “Render not evil for *evil*;” it is “Whoso *smiteth thee on one cheek*.” All this supposes previous offence, or injury, or violence; and it is *then* that forbearance is enjoined.

===Chinese===

整个论证的谬误似乎在于：它假定自然本能是一条具有「至高无上」权威的法则。上帝在人类机体中植入了各种倾向或本能，其意图是充满智慧的。这些倾向在其本性上极易走向「滥用」；当得到过度放纵或盲从时，它们便会颠覆植入它们的智慧旨意，并毁灭人类的福祉。因此，上帝确立了一条由其直接权威所认可的「更高法则」：依凭此法则，我们被要求节制这些倾向。因此，问题不在于自我保存的本能是否由天性植入，而在于基督教是否限制了它的运作。这个问题必须借此、且唯有借此来加以裁决。凡不辞劳苦进行深究之人皆会发现，对自然本能的规范以及对其施展的限制，乃是基督教道德的一个显著宗旨；我认为显而易见的是，这种规范与限制同样适用于摆在我们面前的这一本能。某些此类倾向必须受到克制，这是确凿无疑的。在我们天性中最强烈的本能之一，便是一种基督教规诫特别予以规范的情欲。我并非主张任何自然本能必须被根除，而是主张所有本能都必须受到规范与克制；我对自我保存的本能亦持此主张。

确立这一论点，正是本项探讨的核心目标。自我保存的本能所激发的性情与行动，究竟是何物？不正是基督教所明令禁止的行动与性情吗？它们就是不肯容忍、奋起抵抗与报复伤害。事实在于，基督教和平的训诫所针对的正是「防御」。「侵犯」本身似乎根本未曾被设想过。经上说：「不要与恶人作对」；「以善胜恶」；「待恨你们的人要好」；「爱你们的仇敌」；「不要以恶报恶」；「有人打你的右脸」。这一切皆预设了先前的侵犯、伤害或强暴；而正是在「此时」，宽容与克制才被吩咐下来。

===Original===

“The chief aim,” says a judicious author, “of those who argue in behalf of defensive war, is directed *at the passions*;”60 and accordingly, the case of an assassin will doubtless be brought against me. I shall be asked﻿—Suppose a ruffian breaks into your house, and rushes into your room with his arm lifted to murder you, do you not believe that Christianity allows you to kill him? This is the last refuge of the cause: my answer to it is explicit﻿—*I do not believe it.*

I have referred to this utmost possible extremity, because I am willing to meet objections of whatever nature, and because, by stating this, which is enforced by all our prejudices and all our instincts, I shall at least show, that I can give to those who differ from me, a fair, an open, and a candid recognition of all the consequences of my principles. I would, however, beg the same candor of the reader, and remind him, that were they unable to abide this test, the case of the ruffian has little practical reference to war. I remind him of this, not because I doubt whether our principles can be supported, but because, if he should think that in this case I do not support them, he will yet recollect that very few wars are proved to be lawful.﻿—Of the wars which are prosecuted, some are simply wars of aggression; some are for the maintenance of a balance of power; some are in assertion of technical rights, and some, undoubtedly, to repel invasion. The last are perhaps the fewest; and of these only it can be said that they bear any analogy whatever to the case which is supposed; and even in these, the analogy is seldom complete. It has rarely indeed happened that wars have been undertaken simply for the preservation of life, and that no other alternative has remained to a people, than to kill or to be killed. And let it be remembered, that *unless this alternative only remains*, the case of the ruffian is irrelevant; it applies not, practically, to the subject.

===Chinese===

「一位明智的著述家曾言：『为自卫战争辩护之人的主要意图，乃是直指激情；』60 据此，刺客的例证无疑会被拿来反驳我。有人会质问我：假定一个恶徒破门而入闯进你的宅邸，举起手臂冲入你的房间要将你谋杀，难道你不相信基督教允许你杀了他吗？这是这一立场的最后避难所：我的回答是明确的——我不相信如此。

我之所以提及这种极端的可能性，是因为我愿意直面任何性质的反驳；且由于陈述这种受到我们全部偏见与全部本能所强化的情形，我至少能够表明，自己可以向持不同观点者公正、坦白且坦率地承认我所持原则的一切后果。然而，我恳请读者亦抱持同样的坦诚，并提醒他：即便原则经不起这一检验，恶徒的例证在实际中亦与战争罕有关联。我向他提醒这一点，并非因为我怀疑我们的原则是否站得住脚，而是因为倘若他认为在此例中我未能维护这些原则，他仍会忆及，极少有战争被证明是正当合法的。——在付诸实施的战争中，有些纯粹是侵略战争；有些是为了维持势力均衡；有些是为了主张形式上的技术性权利；而有些则无疑是为了击退入侵。最后一类战争或许数量最少；且唯有这类战争，可以说与所设想的情形存在某种类比关系；即便在这些战争中，这种类比亦极少是完备的。确实极少发生过战争纯粹是为了保全生命而发动、一个民族除了杀人或被杀之外别无选择的先例。务必牢记：除非仅剩下这一抉择，否则恶徒的例证便是无关紧要的；它在实际中并不适用于这一课题。

===Original===

I do not know what those persons mean, who say, that we are authorized to kill an assassin by *the law of nature*. Principles like this, heedlessly assumed, as of self-evident truth, are, I believe, often the starting-post of our errors, the point of divergency from rectitude, from which our after obliquities proceed. Some men seem to talk of the laws of nature, as if nature were a legislatress who had sat and framed laws for the government of mankind. Nature makes no laws; a law implies a legislator; and there is no legislator upon the principles of human duty, but God. If, by the “law of nature,” is meant anything of which the sanctions or obligations are *different* from those of revelation, it is obvious that we have set up a moral system of our own, and in opposition to that which has been established by Heaven. If we mean by “the law of nature,” nothing but that which is *accordant* with revelation, to what purpose do we refer to it at all? I do not suppose that any sober moralist will statedly advance the laws of nature in opposition to the laws of God; but I think that to advance them *at all*﻿—that to refer to *any* principle or law, in determination of our duty, irrespectively of the simple will of God, is always dangerous: for there will be many, who, when they are referred for direction to such law or principle, will regard it, in their practice, as a *final* standard of truth. I believe that a reference to the laws of nature has seldom illustrated our duties, and never induced us to perform them; and that it has hitherto answered little other purpose than that of amusing the lovers of philosophical morality.

===Chinese===

我不知道那些声称我们依凭「自然法」便有权诛杀刺客的人究竟意指为何。依我之见，像这样未经深思便被假定为不证自明的真理之原则，往往正是我们谬误的发端，是偏离正道的分岔点，我们随后的一切偏邪皆由此滋生。有些人谈及自然法，仿佛大自然是一位端坐并制定法律以统治人类的女性立法者。大自然并不制定法则；法则必然预设了立法者；而在关乎人类本分的原则上，除上帝之外绝无立法者。倘若「自然法」所指的是其制裁或义务「有别于」神圣启示的任何事物，显而易见，我们便是建立了一套属于我们自己的道德体系，且与上天所确立的体系背道而驰。倘若我们所指的「自然法」无非是与启示「相契合」的事物，那我们究竟因何还要援引它？我不认为任何清醒的道德学家会公然提出自然法来对抗上帝的法则；但我认为，只要「提出」它们——在决定我们的本分时，不论援引「何种」脱离上帝纯全旨意的原则或法则，始终都是危险的：因为将有许多人在被指引向此类法则或原则寻求准则时，会在践行中将其视为真理的「终极」标准。我相信，援引自然法极少能阐明我们的本分，且从未能促使我们去履行这些本分；迄今为止，除取悦哲学道德的爱好者之外，它几乎未曾起到任何其他作用。

===Original===

The mode of proving, or of stating, the right to kill an assassin, is this:﻿—“There is one case in which all extremities are justifiable; namely, when our life is assaulted, and it becomes necessary for our preservation to kill the assailant. This is evident in a state of nature; unless it can be shown that we are bound to prefer the aggressor’s life to our own; that is to say, to love our enemy *better* than ourselves, which can never be a debt of justice, nor anywhere appears to be a duty of charity.”61 If I were disposed to hold argumentation like this, I would say, that although we may not be required to love our enemies *better* than ourselves, we are required to love them *as* ourselves; and that in the supposed case, it still would be a question equally balanced, which life ought to be sacrificed; for it is quite clear, that if we kill the assailant, we love him *less* than ourselves, which may, perhaps, militate a little against “a duty of charity.” But the truth is, that the question is not whether we should love our enemy better than ourselves, but whether we should sacrifice the laws of Christianity in order to preserve our lives﻿—whether we should prefer the interests of religion to our own﻿—whether we should be willing to “lose our life, for Christ’s sake and the Gospel’s.”

===Chinese===

证明或表述杀戮刺客之权利的方式如下：「有一种情形下，一切极端手段皆属正当；亦即，当我们的生命受到袭击，且为了自我保全而必须杀死袭击者时。这在自然状态下是显而易见的；除非能够表明我们有义务珍视侵犯者的生命甚于自己的生命；换言之，爱我们的仇敌『胜过』爱我们自己，但这绝非正义的本分，在任何地方也都不曾显现为仁爱的义务。」61 倘若我愿意采用此类论辩方式，我便会说：尽管我们或许未被要求爱仇敌「胜过」爱自己，我们却被要求爱他们「如同」爱自己；而在所设想的情形中，究竟应当牺牲哪一方的生命，仍然是一个势均力敌的问题；因为十分显然的是，倘若我们杀死了袭击者，我们爱他便「不及」爱我们自己，这或许多少有悖于「仁爱的义务」。然而事实在于，问题并不在于我们是否应当爱仇敌胜过爱自己，而在于我们是否应当为了保全自己的生命而牺牲基督教的律法——在于我们是否应当将信仰的利益置于我们自身的利益之上——在于我们是否应当甘愿「为基督和福音的缘故丧掉生命」。

===Original===

This system of *counter-crime* is of very loose tendency. The assailant violates his duties by attempting to kill me, and I, therefore, am to violate mine by actually killing him. Is his meditated crime, then, a justification of my perpetrated crime? In the case of a condemned Christian martyr who was about to be led to the stake, it is supposable, that by having contrived a mine, he may preserve his life by suddenly firing it and blowing his persecutors into the air. Would Christianity justify the act? Or what should we say of him if he committed it? We should say that whatever his *faith* might be, his *practice* was very unsound; that he might *believe* the Gospel, but that he certainly did not fulfil its duties. Now I contend that for all the purposes of the argument, the cases of the martyr and the assaulted person are precisely similar. He who was about to be led to the stake, and he who was about to lose his life by the assassin, are both required to regulate their conduct by the same laws, and are both to be prepared to offer up their lives in testimony of their allegiance to Christianity: the one in allegiance to her, in opposition to the violation of her moral principles and her moral spirit; and the other, in opposition to errors in belief or to ecclesiastical corruptions. It is therefore in vain to tell me that the victim of persecution would have suffered for religion’s sake, for so also would the victim of the ruffian. There is nothing in the sanctions of Christianity which implies that obedience to her moral law is of less consequence than an adherence to her faith; nor, as it respects the welfare of the world, does the consequence appear to be less; for he who, by his fidelity to Christianity, promotes the diffusion of Christian dispositions and of peace, contributes, perhaps, as much to the happiness of mankind, as he who by the same fidelity recommends the acceptance of an accurate creed.

A great deal hangs upon this question, and it is therefore necessary to pursue it farther. We say, then, first﻿—that Christianity has not declared that we are ever at liberty to kill other men: secondly﻿—that she virtually prohibits it, because her principles and the practice of our Saviour are not compatible with it: and, thirdly﻿—that if Christianity allowed it, she would in effect and in practice allow *war*, without restriction to defence of life.

===Chinese===

这种「以罪抗罪」的体系具有极其涣散的倾向。袭击者企图杀害我，违背了他的本分，因此我也要通过实际杀死他来违背我的本分。难道他预谋的罪行，竟能成为我犯下实际罪行的正当理由吗？设想一位被定罪的基督徒殉道者即将被引向火刑柱，他设法布置了一处地雷，通过突然引爆将迫害他的人炸上天空来保全自己的性命。基督教是否会认可这一行为？倘若他真做出此举，我们又该如何评价他？我们必定会说：不论他的「信仰」如何，他的「行持」皆极不纯正；他或许「相信」福音，但他断然没有履行福音的本分。如今我坚决主张：就论证的一切目的而言，殉道者的情形与遭受袭击者的情形完全相同。那即将被引向火刑柱的人，与那即将丧命于刺客之手的人，皆被要求依照相同的法则规范自身的行止，且皆应准备献出生命以见证自己对基督教的效忠：前者是为了效忠于她、反对违背她的道德原则与道德精神；后者则是为了反对信仰上的谬误或教会的败坏。因此，对我说受迫害的受害者是为宗教缘故受难，纯属徒劳；因为恶徒手下的受害者同样亦是如此。在基督教的诫命约束中，绝无任何内容暗示顺服其道德律的重要性不及坚持其信仰；且就世间的福祉而言，其重要性亦未见逊色；因为那以对基督教的忠诚促进基督徒性情与和平之传播的人，对人类幸福的贡献，或许丝毫不亚于那以同样的忠诚劝勉人们接受纯正信条的人。

诸多关键悬于此问题之上，因此有必要作进一步探究。于是我们主张：其一——基督教从未宣告我们有权杀戮他人；其二——她实质上禁止杀戮，因为她的原则以及救主的实践皆与此不相容；其三——倘若基督教允许杀戮，她在实质与实践中便会允许「战争」，且绝不仅限于保全生命的防御。

===Original===

The first of these positions will probably not be disputed; and upon the second, that Christianity virtually prohibits the destruction of human life, it has been the principal object of this essay to insist. I would, therefore, only observe, that the conduct of the Founder of Christianity, when his *enemies approached him* “*with swords and staves*,” appears to apply strictly to self-defence. These armed men came with the final purpose of murdering him; but although he knew this purpose, he would not suffer the assailants to be killed or even to be wounded. Christ, therefore, would not preserve his own life by sacrificing another’s.

===Chinese===

这几项论点中的第一项大概不会引起争议；至于第二项，即基督教实质上禁止毁灭人类生命，这一直是本篇论文坚持阐述的主要宗旨。因此，我仅需指出：当基督教创立者的「仇敌带着刀棒向他逼近」时，他的举止显然完全适用于自卫的情形。这些武装人员前来的最终目的是要将他杀害；但他尽管深知这一企图，却不容许袭击者被杀害，甚至不容许他们受到伤害。因此，基督绝不以牺牲他人的生命来保全自己的生命。

===Original===

But we say, thirdly, that if Christianity allows us to kill one another in self-defence, she allows *war*, without restriction to self-defence. Let us try what would have been the result if the Christian Scriptures had thus placed human life at our disposal: suppose they had said﻿—*You may kill a ruffian in your own defence, but you may not enter into a defensive war.* The prohibition would admit, not of *some* exceptions to its application﻿—the exceptions would be so many, that no prohibition would be left; because there is no practical limit to the right of self-defence, until we arrive at defensive war. If one man may kill one, two may kill two, and ten may kill ten, and an army may kill an army:﻿—and this is defensive *war*. Supposing, again, the Christian Scriptures had said, *an army may fight in its own defence, but not for any other purpose*. We do not say that the exceptions to *this* rule would be so many as wholly to nullify the rule itself; but we say that whoever will attempt to apply it in practice, will find that he has a very wide range of justifiable warfare; a range that will embrace many more wars than moralists, laxer than we shall suppose him to be, are willing to defend. If an army may fight in defence of their own lives, they may and must fight in defence of the lives of others: if they may fight in defence of the lives of others, they will fight in defence of their property: if in defence of property, they will fight in defence of political rights: if in defence of rights, they will fight in promotion of interests: if in promotion of interests, they will fight in promotion of their glory and their crimes. Now let any man of honesty look over the gradations by which we arrive at this climax, and I believe he will find that, *in practice*, no curb can be placed upon the conduct of an army until they reach it. There is, indeed, a wide distance between fighting in defence of life, and fighting in furtherance of our crimes; but the steps which lead from one to the other will follow in inevitable succession. I know that the letter of our rule excludes it, but I know the rule will be a letter only.

===Chinese===

然而我们从第三点指出，倘若基督教允许我们在自卫中自相残杀，她便允许了「战争」，且绝不仅限于自卫。让我们审视一下，倘若基督教经书将人类生命交由我们自行处置，结果将会如何：假定经书曾经说过——「为了自卫你可以杀死恶徒，但你不可卷入自卫战争。」该禁令在其适用中容许的将不仅是「某些」例外——例外将会多到不再留下任何禁令；因为在推及自卫战争之前，自卫权在实际中根本不存在任何界限。倘若一人可以杀一人，两人便可以杀两人，十人便可以杀十人，而一支军队便可以杀灭另一支军队：——而这正是自卫「战争」。再次假定，基督教经书曾经说过：「一支军队可以为了自卫而作战，但不得出于任何其他目的。」我们并非说对「这条」规则的例外会多到将规则本身彻底废止；但我们断言，凡试图在实践中应用它的人，皆会发现自己拥有了极其宽泛的正当战事范围；这一范围所涵盖的战争，将远甚于那些比我们所设想的道德学家更为宽纵之人所愿意辩护的限度。倘若一支军队可以为了保卫自身生命而战，他们便可以且必须为了保卫他人的生命而战；倘若他们可以为了保卫他人的生命而战，他们便会为了保卫财产而战；倘若为了保卫财产，他们便会为了保卫政治权利而战；倘若为了保卫权利，他们便会为了促进利益而战；倘若为了促进利益，他们便会为了宣扬自身的荣耀与罪愆而战。如今，让任何诚实之人审视通向这一顶点的层层递进，我相信他会发现，在「实践」中，在军队抵达这一顶点之前，根本没有任何缰绳能够约束其行径。在为保全生命而战与为助长我们的罪愆而战之间，确实相距甚远；然而从前者通向后者的台阶，却会无可避免地接踵而至。我深知我们规则的字面条文排除了后者，但我深知这规则将沦为一纸空文。

===Original===

It is very easy for us to sit in our studies, and to point the commas, and semicolons, and periods of the soldier’s career; it is very easy for us to say he shall stop at defence of life or at protection of property, or at the support of rights; but armies will never listen to us﻿—we shall be only the Xerxes of morality throwing our idle chains into the tempestuous ocean of slaughter.

What is the testimony of experience? When nations are mutually exasperated, and armies are levied, and battles are fought, does not everyone know that with whatever motives of defence one party may have begun the contest, both, in turn, become aggressors? In the fury of slaughter, soldiers do not attend, they cannot attend, to questions of aggression. Their business is destruction, and their business they will perform. If the army of defence obtains success, it soon becomes an army of aggression. Having repelled the invader, it begins to punish him. If a war is once begun, it is vain to think of distinctions of aggression and defence. Moralists may *talk* of distinctions, but soldiers will *make* none; and none can be made; it is without the limits of possibility.

===Chinese===

我们安坐于书斋之中，为军旅生涯标点逗号、分号与句号，是极其轻而易举的事；我们轻而易举地宣称军人应当止步于保卫生命，或止步于保护财产，或止步于维护权利；然而军队绝不会听从我们——我们不过是道德上的薛西斯（Xerxes），将虚妄的锁链抛入狂暴的杀戮之洋中。

经验的见证又是如何？当各民族彼此激怒、征召军队、战火燃起之时，难道人人都不知道，不论一方最初带着何种防御动机开启战端，双方最终都会轮番成为侵略者吗？在杀戮的狂怒之中，士兵们不会、亦无法去顾及何为侵略的问题。他们的行当就是毁灭，而他们势必会履行这一行当。防御的军队一旦取得胜利，便会迅速沦为侵略的军队。在击退入侵者之后，它便着手予以惩罚。战端一旦开启，去空谈侵略与防御的区别纯属徒劳。道德学家或许会「谈论」这些区分，但士兵绝不会「做出」任何区分；而且根本无法做出区分；这完全超出了可能性的界限。

===Original===

But, indeed, what is defensive war? A celebrated moralist defines it to be, war undertaken in consequence of “*an injury* perpetrated, attempted or feared,” which shows with sufficient clearness how little *the assassin* concerns the question, for fear respecting life does not enter into the calculation of “injuries.” So, then, if we fear some injury to our purses, or to our “honor,” we are allowed to send an army to the country that gives us fear, and to slaughter its inhabitants; and this, we are told, is defensive war. By this system of reasoning, which has been happily called “martial logic,” there will be little difficulty in proving any war to be defensive.
